\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{S}_{i}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{g}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\tilde{\bm{g}}}\|_{F}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{S}_{i}\|_{F}\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{g}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\tilde{\bm{g}}}\|_{\text{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{g}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\tilde{\bm{g}}}\|_{\text{op}}),

where the first and second
inequalities
follow from the definition of the operator norm, and the last one follows from the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

By
[
EK10
, Theorem 2.1]
,
\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{g}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\tilde{\bm{g}}}\|_{\text{op}}\to_{P}0
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle|{\bm{v}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbb{E}}\mathbf{R}_{0}{\bm{w}}-{\bm{v}}^{\top}\mathbb{E}\tilde{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{w}}|\leq O_{\mathbb{P}}(\left\|\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{g}}-\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\tilde{\bm{g}}}\right\|_{\text{op}})+O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}})\to_{P}0.

Appendix J
Proofs of Results from Section
4.2

Here, we will prove the results in Section
4.2
. First, we will provide several lemmas, which will be used in our proofs. The first lemma allows us to approximate linear and quadratic forms of
\boldsymbol{\beta}
in terms of
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}
; the quadratic form result is from
[
BES+22
]
. Its proof is in Section
N.2
.

Lemma J.1
.

For any
d\in\mathbb{N}
,
let
\mathbf{v}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}

be
vectors and matrices,
fixed or independent of
\mathbf{X},\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star},\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n}
, and
satisfy
\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2},\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\textnormal{op}}\leq C

almost surely,
uniformly for some constant
C>0
.
Under Condition
2.1
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}-c_{\star,1}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\right|\to 0,\quad\left|\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\boldsymbol{\beta}-\frac{1}{n}(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})\operatorname{tr}\mathbf{D}-c_{\star,1}^{2}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\mathbf{D}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\right|\to 0

in probability as
d\to\infty
.

We will use the expression derived for the training loss in the following lemma; see Section
N.3
for the proof.

Lemma J.2
.

The training loss
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})
can be written as

{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})=\lambda\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{y}}
.

The following lemma will be used in proving concentration of certain quadratic forms appearing in the proofs; see Section
N.4

for the proof.

Lemma J.3
.

Let
g:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
be a polynomial,
\mathbf{D}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
be a matrix
with
\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/n)
, and
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a vector of i.i.d. Gaussian random variables with bounded variance independent of
\mathbf{D}
. We have

\displaystyle\Big|g(\mathbf{Z})^{\top}\mathbf{D}\;g(\mathbf{Z})-\mathbb{E}[g(\mathbf{Z})^{\top}\mathbf{D}\;g(\mathbf{Z})]\Big|\to_{P}0,

in which
g
is applied elementwise.

The limiting values of two key quadratic forms appearing in the proof are derived in the following lemma, whose proof is deferred to Section
N.5
.

Lemma J.4
.

Let
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
be the solutions to the system of fixed point equations from equation
4.2
.
Then, the following holds:

(a)

\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}=\psi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})m_{2}+\frac{\psi}{\phi}c_{\star,1}^{2}m_{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

(b)

{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}=-\lambda\frac{\psi^{2}}{\phi^{2}}m_{1}+\frac{\psi}{\phi}-1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

In particular,
\psi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})m_{2}+\frac{\psi}{\phi}c_{\star,1}^{2}m_{2}\neq 0
and
-\lambda\frac{\psi^{2}}{\phi^{2}}m_{1}+\frac{\psi}{\phi}-1\neq 0
.

The following lemmas will be used in the computations.
We defer the proofs of these lemmas to Sections
N.6
,
N.7
,

N.8
,
and
N.9
respectively.

Lemma J.5
.

For any
p,q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
p\neq q
and any vector
{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, with
\|{\bm{u}}\|_{2}=1
independent of
\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
, we have
H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Lemma J.6
.

For any
p\in\mathbb{N}
, we have

(a)

\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,

(b)

\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Lemma J.7
.

For
s\in\{1,2\}
,
p\in\mathbb{N}
,
and
p\neq s
, we have

H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ s}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Further,

\displaystyle\lim_{n,N,d\to\infty}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}=2c_{\star,1}^{2}\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi}.

Lemma J.8
.

We have

\displaystyle\lim_{n,N,d\to\infty}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}=\frac{3\psi m_{1}}{\phi}[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}.

Now, we will first provide a proof of Theorem
4.3
in the case of
\ell=1
and
\ell=2
for a better insight into the proof techniques. We will then prove the general form in Section
K
.

J.1
Proof for
\ell=1

In the
\ell=1
regime,
due to Theorem
4.1
, we can replace
\mathbf{F}
by
\mathbf{F}_{1}
(defined in equation
4
) to compute the training loss.
Hence, from now on we let
\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{F}_{1}
. We can write

\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}=\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{U}^{\top}

where

\mathbf{U}=[\,\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}\;|\;\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\;]
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}=\begin{bmatrix}0&c_{1}^{2}\eta\\
c_{1}^{2}\eta&c_{1}^{4}\eta^{2}\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix}.

Based on Lemma
J.2
,
the training loss depends on

\bar{\mathbf{R}}=(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}
.
Using the Woodbury formula, this matrix can be written
in terms of
\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}

as

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{R}}=\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}(\mathbf{K}^{-1}+\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U})^{-1}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}.

(24)

Defining
\mathbf{T}=(\mathbf{K}^{-1}+\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U})^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\times 2}

and substituting

\bar{\mathbf{R}}=\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}

in the formula for training loss in Lemma
J.2
, we find

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})=\lambda\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}.

(25)

Using equation
25

and

\mathbf{U}=[\,\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}\;|\;\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\;]
,
the loss difference can be written as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})

\displaystyle-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle=\lambda\biggl[T_{11}(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{2}+(T_{12}+T_{21})\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\cdot{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}+T_{22}(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}\biggr],

(26)

in which
T_{ij}
are the elements of the matrix
\mathbf{T}
.
Using

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}=\frac{\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}&-\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}-{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}~\vskip 4.26773pt\\
-\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}&{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)\left({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}+{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)^{2}},

(27)

we will compute the limit of each term appearing in equation
J.1
separately:

Term 1.

The first term can be written as

\displaystyle\delta_{1}

\displaystyle=\lambda T_{11}(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{2}=\frac{\lambda\,(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\,(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{2}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)\left({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}+{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)^{2}}.

Based on Lemma
J.4
, we know that

\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}
and
{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}
are
\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Also, it can easily be seen that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{F}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{op}}\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{op}}\|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Hence,

\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}+{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

(28)

because
{\bm{a}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}
. Also,
using that

\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}=(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}=\mathbf{I}-\lambda n\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
,

\displaystyle(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{2}=\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbb{E}_{\bm{a}}[{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}]\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\frac{1}{N}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}-\frac{\lambda n}{N}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}^{2}\tilde{\bm{y}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where in the last inequality, we used that
\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}\leq\frac{1}{\lambda n}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}^{2}\tilde{\bm{y}}\leq\frac{1}{(\lambda n)^{2}}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Putting everything together, it follows that
\delta_{1}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
in probability.

Term 2 and Term 3.

The second and third terms can be written as

\displaystyle\delta_{2}

\displaystyle=\delta_{3}=\lambda T_{12}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda\,\left(-\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}-{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)\,(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}})}{\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)\left({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}+{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)^{2}}.

Recall from the above argument that the denominator is
\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and that
\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}+{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Also,
\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}\leq\frac{1}{(\lambda n)^{2}}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}\|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{F}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Therefore, we find
\delta_{2}=\delta_{3}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Term 4.

This term can be written as

\displaystyle\delta_{4}=\lambda T_{22}(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\lambda\,\left({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\right)\,(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\left({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}+{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)^{2}}=\lambda\frac{(\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

since
\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}+{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}=\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\neq 0
by Lemma
J.4
.
By equation
1
and Condition
2.4
, we can write
\tilde{\bm{y}}=\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}c_{\star,p}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
,
where
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is additive Gaussian noise.
Note that

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}

\displaystyle=c_{\star,1}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

by Lemma
J.7
and since
\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
and
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}
.

Further by Lemma
J.1
,

\displaystyle c_{\star,1}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}=c_{\star,1}^{2}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\to_{P}c_{\star,1}^{2}\frac{\psi}{\phi}m_{2},

(29)

where the final limit follows from the proof of Lemma
J.4
. By summing up the fours terms computed above and using Lemma
J.4
, we find

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})\to_{P}\Delta_{1}=\frac{\psi\lambda c_{\star,1}^{4}m_{2}}{\phi[c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})]}>0,

(30)

which concludes the proof for
\ell=1
.

J.2
Proof for
\ell=2

In the
\ell=2
regime, based on Theorem
4.1
, we can replace
\mathbf{F}
with
\mathbf{F}_{2}
(defined in equation
4
) to compute the training loss. Hence, from now on we let
\mathbf{F}=\mathbf{F}_{2}
. We can write

\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}=\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{U}^{\top}

where

\mathbf{U}=[\,\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}\;|\;\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\sqrt{N}\;|\;\;\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\;|\;(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\;]
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}=\begin{bmatrix}0&0&c_{1}^{2}\eta&0\\[8.5359pt]
0&0&0&c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\eta^{2}/\sqrt{N}\\[8.5359pt]
c_{1}^{2}\eta&0&c_{1}^{4}\eta^{2}\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}&c_{1}^{4}c_{2}\eta^{3}\langle{\bm{a}},{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\rangle\\[8.5359pt]
0&c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\eta^{2}/\sqrt{N}&c_{1}^{4}c_{2}\eta^{3}\langle{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2},{\bm{a}}\rangle&c_{1}^{4}c_{2}^{2}\eta^{4}\langle{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2},{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\rangle\end{bmatrix}.

Recalling

\bar{\mathbf{R}}=(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}

and

\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}
,
we still have equation
24
.
Defining

\mathbf{T}=(\mathbf{K}^{-1}+\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U})^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{4\times 4}
, we have the following analogue to equation
25
:

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})=\lambda\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}.

(31)

Denoting in what follows

\mathbf{Q}=\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}
,
the inverse
\mathbf{T}^{-1}
can be written as follows:

\displaystyle\scalebox{0.9}{\mbox{$\displaystyle\hskip-14.22636pt\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}&N^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}&{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}+\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}&{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\\[14.22636pt]
N^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{Q}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}&{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{Q}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}-N\|{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}^{2}&N^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}&N^{\frac{1}{2}}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}+\frac{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}{c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\eta^{2}}\\[14.22636pt]
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}+\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}&N^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}&\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}&\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\\[14.22636pt]
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}&N^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}+\frac{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}{c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\eta^{2}}&\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}&\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\end{bmatrix}$}}.

(32)

J.2.1
Analysis of Terms in
\mathbf{T}^{-1}
and
\mathbf{T}

In the following section, we will first analyze the elements of
\mathbf{T}^{-1}
:

(1,1):

The term
{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}
has already been analyzed in Lemma
J.4
and is
\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

(1,2) and (2,1):

Recalling
\mathbf{Q}=\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}
and

\mathbf{R}_{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}
,
we can write

\displaystyle[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{1,2}=[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,1}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{Q}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}-\sqrt{N}\langle{\bm{a}},{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\rangle

\displaystyle=-\lambda n\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=-\lambda n\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{0}\left({\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}-1/N\mathbf{1}_{N}+1/N\mathbf{1}_{N}\right).

Introducing
\tilde{\bm{a}}=\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{N})
,
and as
H_{2}(x)=x^{2}-1
for all
x
,
we find

\displaystyle[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{1,2}=[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,1}=-\frac{\lambda n}{N}\tilde{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{a}})-\frac{\lambda n}{\sqrt{N}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}.

The second term converges to zero as
n\to\infty
because
{\bm{a}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{I}_{N})
is independent of
\mathbf{R}_{0}
, and

\bigl\|\frac{n}{\sqrt{N}}\mathbf{R}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}\bigr\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Moreover, the first term also converges to zero; indeed,

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{a}})=\left(\frac{\tilde{\bm{a}}^{\top}+H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{a}})}{2}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{0}\left(\frac{\tilde{\bm{a}}^{\top}+H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{a}})}{2}\right)-\left(\frac{\tilde{\bm{a}}^{\top}-H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{a}})}{2}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{0}\left(\frac{\tilde{\bm{a}}^{\top}-H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{a}})}{2}\right).

Lemma
J.3
can be used with
\mathbf{D}=\mathbf{R}
to prove the concentration of both term around their expectation. Note that the expectation of
\tilde{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{R}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{a}})
is zero because of the orthogonality property of Hermite polynomials and the independence of
\tilde{\bm{a}}
and
\mathbf{R}_{0}
.
Putting everything together, we conclude that
[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{1,2}=[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,1}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

(1,3) and (3,1):

Recalling that
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}
,
it follows from equation
28
that this term is
o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
.

(1,4) and (4,1):

To bound
{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}
, note that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\|_{\rm op}\leq\|\mathbf{F}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\|\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Hence, because
{\bm{a}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{I}_{N})
is independent of
\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}
, we have

\displaystyle[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{1,4}=[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{4,1}={\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(2,2):

This term is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, because
{\bm{a}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{I}_{N})
, so

\displaystyle[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,2}

\displaystyle={N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{Q}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}-N\|{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}^{2}=-\lambda Nn\,{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}

\displaystyle\leq\lambda Nn\,\|{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}^{2}\cdot\|(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(2,3) and (3,2):

To bound
\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}
, note that

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}\|\mathbf{F}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\|\mathbf{R}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm op}\leq C\cdot\sqrt{N}\cdot\frac{1}{n}\cdot\sqrt{N}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Also, by Lemma
J.1
, we have

\displaystyle[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,3}=[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{3,2}=\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}=c_{\star,1}\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

which converges to zero, because
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d})
and is independent of
\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
, which has bounded norm in probability.

(2,4) and (4,2):

First note that in the regime where
\ell=2
, we have
\frac{\sqrt{N}}{\eta^{2}}\to 0
.
Hence, we can write

\displaystyle[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,4}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{N}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\sqrt{N}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}+\sqrt{N}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(33)

By Lemma
J.6
, the first term converges in probability to zero.
Moreover,
{\bm{a}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{I}_{N})
is independent of
\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}
,
and
\|\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Thus,
we have that
\sqrt{N}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left({\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}-1/N\mathbf{1}_{N}\right)\to_{P}0
.
Hence, we find

\displaystyle[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,4}=\sqrt{N}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}/N+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Based on the Gaussian equivalence from Appendix
I
, we
can replace
\mathbf{F}_{0}
with

\mathbf{F}_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
,
where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
is an independent random matrix with
{\sf N}(0,1)
entries, without changing the limit.
Now, the linearized
\mathbf{F}_{0}
is left-orthogonally invariant, hence
\mathbf{F}_{0}
has the same distribution as
\mathbf{O}\mathbf{F}_{0}
, where
\mathbf{O}
is uniformly distributed over the Haar measure of
d
-dimensional orthogonal matrices, independently of all other randomness.
Hence,

N^{-1/2}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}=_{d}N^{-1/2}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}.

Now,

\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{n}=_{d}\sqrt{n}{\bm{z}}/\|{\bm{z}}\|_{2}
, where
{\bm{z}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\mathbf{I}_{n})
. Moreover
\|{\bm{z}}\|_{2}=\sqrt{n}(1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1))
, hence
replacing
\mathbf{O}^{\top}\mathbf{1}_{n}
with
{\bm{z}}^{\top}
introduces negligible error.
Hence,

[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,4}=_{d}N^{-1/2}{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Now,
{\bm{z}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}\sim{\sf N}(0,\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}\|_{2}^{2})
, and

\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}\|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
,
thus

[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,4}\to_{P}0
.

(3,3):

We have
\|\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})
and
\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/n)
. Thus,
[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{3,3}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

(3,4) and (4,3):

First, note that defining
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}}
,
and as
H_{2}(x)=x^{2}-1
for all
x
,
we can write

\displaystyle[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{3,4}=[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{4,3}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{3}\left((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\circ 2}\right)

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{3}\left((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right)+\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\left(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}\right).

Now, by Lemma
J.1
, we have

\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}=c_{\star,1}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Now, note that
\|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d})
is independent of
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{1}_{N}
, which implies that the second term converges to zero. By using Lemma
J.5
for
{\bm{u}}=\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
,
the first term also converges to zero. Putting these together, we have
[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{3,4}=[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{4,3}=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
.

(4,4):

We have
\|\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})
and
\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/n)
. Thus,
[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{4,4}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Now, putting everything together, the matrix
\mathbf{T}^{-1}
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{1,1}&0&0&0\\[14.22636pt]
0&[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,2}&0&0\\[14.22636pt]
0&0&[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{3,3}&0\\[14.22636pt]
0&0&0&[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{4,4}\end{bmatrix}+\mathbf{\Delta}_{1},

where the all elements of
\mathbf{\Delta}_{1}
are
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Thus the matrix
\mathbf{T}
is equal to

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}=\begin{bmatrix}\frac{1}{[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{1,1}}&0&0&0\\[14.22636pt]
0&\frac{1}{[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{2,2}}&0&0\\[14.22636pt]
0&0&\frac{1}{[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{3,3}}&0\\[14.22636pt]
0&0&0&\frac{1}{[\mathbf{T}^{-1}]_{4,4}}\end{bmatrix}+\mathbf{\Delta}_{2},

(34)

where the all elements of
\mathbf{\Delta}_{2}
are
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

J.2.2
Computing the training loss

Having computed the limit of the matrix
\mathbf{T}^{-1}
and
\mathbf{T}
, we are now ready to put everything together and compute the limiting train loss. One can write the outcome vector
\tilde{\bm{y}}
as
\tilde{\bm{y}}=\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
, where
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
is the noise term. Thus, using equation
31
, we find

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle=\lambda\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+2\lambda\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}+\lambda\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}.

(35)

We will first argue the second and third term will go to zero in probability. To do this, we note that
\|\mathbf{T}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and also
\|\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{2}\leq\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\rm op}\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1/\sqrt{n})
. We have
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\sim{\sf N}(0,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n})
and it is independent of
\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0},\mathbf{U},\mathbf{T},\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
, and
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}
. Also note that
\|\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{2}=O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1).
Thus, the second and third term in equation
J.2.2
go to zero and we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle=\lambda\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

If we expand
\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})
in the Hermite basis as
\sigma_{\star}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})=\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}c_{\star,p}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})
, we can write

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})=\lambda\sum_{p,q=1}^{\infty}c_{\star,p}c_{\star,q}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

We define
\Delta_{p,q}=H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})=\delta_{1}^{p,q}+\delta_{2}^{p,q}+\delta_{3}^{p,q}+\delta_{4}^{p,q}

in which,
with
T_{i,j}
being the
(i,j)
-th elements of the matrix
\mathbf{T}
,

\displaystyle\delta_{1}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=T_{1,1}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{1,2}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\sqrt{N}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{1,3}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{1,4}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}),

(36)

\displaystyle\delta_{2}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=T_{2,1}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\sqrt{N}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{2,2}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\sqrt{N}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})(\sqrt{N}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{2,3}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\sqrt{N}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{2,4}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\sqrt{N}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}),

(37)

\displaystyle\delta_{3}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=T_{3,1}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{3,2}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}(\sqrt{N}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{3,3}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{3,4}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}),

(38)

and

\displaystyle\delta_{4}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=T_{4,1}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{4,2}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}(\sqrt{N}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{4,3}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\vskip 5.69046pt

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+T_{4,4}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}).

(39)

We will now look at each
\delta_{i}^{p,q}
for
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
.

Term
\delta_{1}^{p,q}
:

To prove that the term in equation
J.2.2
are asymptotically negligible, note that
{\bm{a}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{I}_{N})
is independent of
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}
and we have
\|H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\|_{\rm 2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Thus,
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and all other terms multiplying this are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. This implies that for any
p,q\in\mathbb{N}
, we have
\delta_{1}^{p,q}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Term
\delta_{2}^{p,q}
:

All four terms in equation
J.2.2
converge to zero. To prove this, we will use the Lemma
J.6
. In equation
J.2.2
, all terms multiplied by
\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}
are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Thus,
\delta_{2}^{p,q}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
for any
p,q\in\mathbb{N}
.

Term
\delta_{3}^{p,q}
:

The first term in equation
J.2.2
converges to zero in probability due to an argument similar to the arguments used for
\delta_{1}^{p,q}
;
and the same holds for the second term in equation
J.2.2
,
by arguing similarly as for
\delta_{2}^{p,q}
.
We have shown that
T_{3,4}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and by a norm argument, we can see that
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}
and
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})
are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Hence,

\displaystyle\delta_{3}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=T_{3,3}\bigl(H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\bigr)\bigl(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bigr)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Term
\delta_{4}^{p,q}
:

The first two terms in equation
J.2.2
converge to zero by the same reasoning used for
\delta_{1}^{p,q}
and
\delta_{2}^{p,q}
, respectively.
The third term can also be shown to converge to zero by recalling that
T_{4,3}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Hence, we can write

\displaystyle\delta_{4}^{p,q}

\displaystyle=T_{4,4}\bigl(H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\bigr)\bigl(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bigr)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Putting everything together, we find

\displaystyle L_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-L_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle=\lambda T_{3,3}\sum_{p,q=1}^{\infty}c_{\star,p}c_{\star,q}(H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\bigr)\bigl(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bigr)

\displaystyle+\lambda T_{4,4}\sum_{p,q=1}^{\infty}c_{\star,p}c_{\star,q}\bigl(H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\bigr)\bigl(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bigr)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Using Lemma
J.7
, we know that in the sums above, the terms corresponding to
(p,q)=(1,1)
and
(p,q)=(2,2)
are the only non-negligible terms in the first and second sum respectively.

Hence,
as
T_{3,3}=1/{(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

and

T_{4,4}=1/(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2})+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
,
from Lemmas

J.1
,
J.4
,

J.7
and
J.8
,
we can write,

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})

\displaystyle=\lambda T_{3,3}c_{\star,1}^{2}\bigl(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\bigr)^{2}+\lambda T_{4,4}c_{\star,2}^{2}\bigl(H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\bigr)^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=\lambda\frac{c_{\star,1}^{2}\bigl(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\bigr)^{2}}{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}}+\lambda c_{\star,2}^{2}\frac{\bigl(H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\bigr)^{2}}{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle\to_{P}\Delta_{2}=\frac{\psi\lambda c_{\star,1}^{4}m_{2}}{\phi[c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})]}+\frac{4\psi\lambda c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}m_{1}}{3\phi[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}},

proving the theorem for
\ell=2
.

Appendix K
Asymptotics of the Training Loss for General
\ell

We define the values
\xi_{i,j}

for all
i,j\in\{0,1,\ldots\}

such that for any
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
x^{p}=\sum_{i=0}^{p}\xi_{p,i}H_{i}(x)
.

Theorem K.1
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
. If Conditions
2.1
-
2.4
hold,
while we also have
c_{1},\cdots,c_{\ell}\neq 0
, and

\eta\asymp n^{\alpha}
with
\frac{\ell-1}{2\ell}<\alpha<\frac{\ell}{2\ell+2}
,
then
for the learned feature map
\mathbf{F}
and the untrained feature map
\mathbf{F}_{0}
, we have
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})\to_{P}\Delta_{\ell}>0
, where

\displaystyle\Delta_{\ell}=\lambda\sum_{p=1}^{\ell}\sum_{q=1}^{\ell}c_{\star,p}c_{\star,q}r_{p}r_{q}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\;\mathbf{\Omega}_{i,j}\left({\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\right)^{(i+j)/2}\xi_{i,p}\xi_{j,q}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

in which
\mathbf{\Omega}
is an invertible matrix with

\displaystyle[\mathbf{\Omega}^{-1}]_{i,j}=\left(c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})\right)^{(i+j)/2}\frac{\psi}{\phi}\left[m_{2}\xi_{i,1}\xi_{j,1}+m_{1}\sum_{k=0,\;k\neq 1}^{\min(i,j)}k!\;\xi_{i,k}\xi_{j,k}\right],\quad\forall i,j\in[\ell],

and for
p\in\mathbb{N}
,

\displaystyle r_{p}=\begin{cases}\frac{p!\psi m_{1}}{\phi}\left(\frac{c_{\star,1}}{\sqrt{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}}\right)^{p}&p\neq 1\\
\frac{\psi m_{2}}{\phi}\frac{c_{\star,1}}{\sqrt{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}}&p=1\end{cases}

Proof of Theorem
K.1
.

In the regime where
\eta\asymp n^{\alpha}
with
\frac{\ell-1}{2\ell}<\alpha<\frac{\ell}{2\ell+2}
, according to the equivalence theorem
4.1
, we can replace
\mathbf{F}
with
\mathbf{F}_{\ell}
when computing the limiting training loss.
To compute the limiting training loss difference according to lemma
J.2
, we study the matrix

\bar{\mathbf{R}}=(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}
.
Due to equation
4
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}=\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{k}c_{k}\eta^{k}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ k}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ k})^{\top}

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{k}c_{k}\eta^{k}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ k})\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ k\top}+\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{i+j}c_{i}c_{j}\eta^{i+j}({\bm{a}}^{\circ i})^{\top}({\bm{a}}^{\circ j})\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ i}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ j\top}.

Defining the matrix
\mathbf{U}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}=\left[\underbrace{\;\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}\;\big|\cdots\big|\;N^{(\ell-1)/2}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ\ell}}_{\ell\text{ columns}}\;\bigg|\underbrace{\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\;\big|\cdots\big|\;\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ\ell}}_{\ell\text{ columns}}\right]\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 2\ell},

we can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}=\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{U}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{U}^{\top},\text{ in which }\mathbf{K}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{0}_{\ell\times\ell}&\mathbf{K}_{o}\\
\mathbf{K}_{o}&\tilde{\mathbf{K}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\ell\times 2\ell},

where
\mathbf{K}_{o}=\text{diag}\left(\frac{c_{1}c_{1}\eta}{N^{0}},\dots,\frac{c_{1}^{\ell}c_{\ell}\eta^{\ell}}{N^{(\ell-1)/2}}\right)\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell},
and
\tilde{\mathbf{K}}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell}
with

[\tilde{\mathbf{K}}]_{i,j}={c_{1}^{i+j}c_{i}c_{j}\eta^{i+j}\langle{\bm{a}}^{\circ i},{\bm{a}}^{\circ j}\rangle}
, for all
i,j\in[\ell]
.

Using the Woodbury formula, the matrix
\bar{\mathbf{R}}
can be written in terms of
\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}

and
\mathbf{T}=(\mathbf{K}^{-1}+\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U})^{-1}\in\mathbb{R}^{2\ell\times 2\ell}
as

\bar{\mathbf{R}}=\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
.
Now

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mathbf{K}}&\mathbf{K}_{o}^{-1}\\
\mathbf{K}_{o}^{-1}&\mathbf{0}_{\ell\times\ell}\end{bmatrix},\text{ where }\quad\mathbf{K}_{o}^{-1}=\text{diag}\left(\frac{N^{0}}{c_{1}c_{1}\eta},\dots,\frac{N^{\frac{\ell-1}{2}}}{c_{1}^{\ell}c_{\ell}\eta^{\ell}}\right),

and
[\hat{\mathbf{K}}]_{i,j}=-N^{(i-1)/2}N^{(j-1)/2}\langle{\bm{a}}^{\circ i},{\bm{a}}^{\circ j}\rangle
, for all
i,j\in[\ell]
.
We define
\mathbf{M}_{1},\mathbf{M}_{2},\mathbf{M}_{o}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell}
as the following blocks of
\mathbf{T}^{-1}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{M}_{1}&\mathbf{M}_{o}\\
\mathbf{M}_{o}&\mathbf{M}_{2}\end{bmatrix}.

Hence, we have

\displaystyle\begin{cases}[\mathbf{M}_{1}]_{i,j}=N^{(i-1)/2}N^{(j-1)/2}{\bm{a}}^{\circ i\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ j},\vskip 8.5359pt\\
[\mathbf{M}_{o}]_{i,j}=N^{(i-1)/2}{\bm{a}}^{\circ i\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ j}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1),\vskip 8.5359pt\\
[\mathbf{M}_{2}]_{i,j}=\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ i\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ j}.\end{cases}

We can expand the monomials in terms of the Hermite polynomials, for scalars
\xi_{i,k}
,
k\in[i]
, as follows:

\displaystyle(N^{1/2}{\bm{a}})^{\circ i}=\sum_{k=0}^{i}\xi_{i,k}H_{k}(N^{1/2}{\bm{a}}),\,\quad\text{and }\quad(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ i}=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\xi_{i,k}H_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}).

Using these, we will analyze each matrix
\mathbf{M}_{1},\mathbf{M}_{2},\mathbf{M}_{o}
separately.

Analysis of
\mathbf{M}_{1}
.

It is easily seen that the elements of this matrix are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Analysis of
\mathbf{M}_{2}
.

To analyze these terms, we need the following lemma, whose proof is deferred to Section
N.10
.

Lemma K.2
.

For any
i,j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ i\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ j}\to_{P}\left(c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})\right)^{(i+j)/2}\left[\xi_{i,1}\xi_{j,1}\frac{\psi m_{2}}{\phi}+\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi}\sum_{k=0,\;k\neq 1}^{\min(i,j)}k!\;\xi_{i,k}\xi_{j,k}\right].

Defining the matrix
\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell}
with entries

\displaystyle[\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}]_{i,j}=\left(c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})\right)^{(i+j)/2}\left[\xi_{i,1}\xi_{j,1}\frac{\psi m_{2}}{\phi}+\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi}\sum_{k=0,\;k\neq 1}^{\min(i,j)}k!\;\xi_{i,k}\xi_{j,k}\right],

for all
i,j\in[\ell]
,
we have
[\mathbf{M}_{2}]_{i,j}\to_{P}[\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}]_{i,j}
. Note that we can write

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}=\frac{\psi}{\phi}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{B}+\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi}{\bm{e}}{\bm{e}}^{\top},

where we define
b=(c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}))^{1/2}
,
\textbf{B}=\text{diag}(b^{1},\cdots,b^{\ell})\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell}
,
{\bm{e}}=\mathbf{B}[\xi_{1,0},\cdots,\xi_{\ell,0}]^{\top}
,

\displaystyle\textbf{M}=\begin{bmatrix}1!\,m_{2}&0&\cdots&0\\
0&2!\,m_{1}&\cdots&0\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
0&0&\cdots&\ell!\,m_{1}\\
\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell},\;\text{and }\;\mathbf{Z}=\begin{bmatrix}\xi_{1,1}&\cdots&\xi_{1,\ell}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\xi_{\ell,1}&\cdots&\xi_{\ell,\ell}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{\ell\times\ell}.

Recalling that
for all
i,j\in\{0,1,\ldots\}
,

\xi_{i,j}
are such that
such that for any
p\in\mathbb{N}
and
x\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
x^{p}=\sum_{i=0}^{p}\xi_{p,i}H_{i}(x)
,
it follows that the matrix
\mathbf{Z}
is lower-triangular with unit diagonal; hence invertible. Thus,
since
\mathbf{B},\mathbf{M}
are diagonal with positive entries,
the matrix
\mathbf{B}\mathbf{Z}\mathbf{M}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}\mathbf{B}
is positive definite. This implies that
\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}
is invertible. We will denote
\mathbf{\Omega}=\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}^{-1}
.

Analysis of
\mathbf{M}_{o}
.

We analyze
[\mathbf{M}_{o}]_{i,j}
by writing
N^{(i-1)/2}{\bm{a}}^{\circ i}
in the Hermite basis, finding

\displaystyle[\mathbf{M}_{o}]_{i,j}=\sum_{k=0}^{i}\frac{\xi_{i,k}}{\sqrt{N}}H_{k}(N^{1/2}{\bm{a}})^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ j}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1).

The terms with
k>0
are all
o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
because
\frac{H_{k}(N^{1/2}{\bm{a}})}{\sqrt{N}}
is a norm
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
vector with mean zero, independent from the vector
\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ j}
with norm
O_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
. Thus,
[\mathbf{M}_{o}]_{i,j}=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
. The term with
k=0
can also be shown to be
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by using that the linearized
\mathbf{F}_{0}
is left-orthogonally invariant, via an argument identical to the one used to analyze equation
33
.

Hence, putting these together, the matrix
\mathbf{T}
can be written as

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}=\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{M}_{1}^{-1}&\mathbf{0}_{\ell\times\ell}\\
\mathbf{0}_{\ell\times\ell}&\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}^{-1}\end{bmatrix}+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1).

Using lemma
J.2
, we can write the training loss difference as

{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})=\lambda{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{y}}
.
Plugging in the teacher function
f_{\star}
, we find

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0})-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q}\lambda c_{\star,p}c_{\star,q}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})

\displaystyle+2\lambda\sum_{p}\left(c_{\star,p}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\right)+\lambda\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}.

Note that the second term can be shown to be
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
because
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\sim{\sf N}(0,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{n})
and it is independent from
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
, and
\|H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{N})
with a simple orderwise analysis. The third can also be shown to be
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by noting that
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
is independent from
\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}
,
\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
and that the elements of
\mathbf{T}
are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

To analyze the first term, we define
\delta_{p,q}=H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})
for all non-negative integers
p,q
. To analyze such terms, we first expand
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}N^{(i+j)/2-1}[\mathbf{M}_{1}^{-1}]_{i,j}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ i})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ j})^{\top}+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}[\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}^{-1}]_{i,j}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ i}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ j\top}.

Thus, for any
p,q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, the terms
\delta_{p,q}
can be written as

\displaystyle\delta_{p,q}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}N^{(i+j)/2-1}[\mathbf{M}_{1}^{-1}]_{i,j}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ i})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ j})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\;[\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}^{-1}]_{i,j}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ i}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ j\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}).

By an argument identical to the argument for the terms in
\mathbf{M}_{o}
, the first sum goes to zero in probability. Denoting
\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}:=\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
, we can expand
(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ i}=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{i}\sum_{k=0}^{i}\xi_{i,k}H_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2})
,
To analyze
\delta_{p,q}
, we need the following result, whose proof is deferred to Section
N.11
.

Lemma K.3
.

For any
p,q\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\,H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\to_{P}\begin{cases}\frac{p!\psi m_{1}}{\phi}\left(\frac{c_{\star,1}}{\sqrt{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}}\right)^{p}&p=q\neq 1\\
\frac{\psi m_{2}}{\phi}\frac{c_{\star,1}}{\sqrt{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}}&p=q=1\\
0&p\neq q.\end{cases}

We can now use Lemma
K.3
and that
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}\to_{P}\left({\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\right)^{1/2}
to write

\displaystyle\delta_{p,q}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\;[\bar{\mathbf{M}}_{2}^{-1}]_{i,j}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{i+j}\xi_{i,p}\xi_{j,q}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\cdot H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}\;[\mathbf{\Omega}]_{i,j}\left({\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\right)^{(i+j)/2}\xi_{i,p}\xi_{j,q}r_{p}r_{q}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

for
p,q\in[\ell]
, which concludes the proof.
∎

Appendix L
Infinite sample limit

In the infinite sample limit, where
n\gg N,d
, we have
\phi\to 0
. In this extreme case, the expressions for
m_{1},m_{2}
will further simplify as
m_{1},m_{2}\to\phi/\lambda\psi
. Note that in this limit, we have
\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{F}_{0})\to\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+c_{\star}^{2}
(see e.g.,
[
MM22
, Section 6]
.
Using Corollary
4.4
, we see that for example when
\ell=2
, we have
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{F})\to\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\frac{2c_{\star,2}^{2}}{3}+c_{\star,>2}^{2}
.
In particular, the term corresponding to the linear component of the teacher function in
\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{F}_{0})
cancels out with the corresponding term in
\Delta_{2}
.

Appendix M
Proof of Theorem
4.5

Let
({\bm{x}}_{\rm te},y_{\rm te})
follow the model from (
1
). Recall that the test error can be written as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}{(\mathbf{F})})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te},y_{\rm te}}(y_{\rm te}-\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}\sigma(\mathbf{W}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}))^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te},y_{\rm te}}(y_{\rm te}^{2})+\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\hat{\bm{a}}-2\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}},

(40)

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left[\sigma(\mathbf{W}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})\sigma(\mathbf{W}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})^{\top}\right]
and
{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te},y_{\rm te}}\left[y_{\rm te}\sigma(\mathbf{W}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})\right]
. First, we will show that in the definition of
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
and
{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}
, we can replace the test feature
\sigma(\mathbf{W}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})
with the spiked approximation from Theorem
3.1

\displaystyle{\bm{f}}_{\ell}=\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{k}c_{k}\eta^{k}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})^{k}{\bm{a}}^{\circ k},

(41)

without changing the test error. To do this, consider an independent test set
\{{\bm{x}}_{{\rm te},i},y_{{\rm te},i}\}_{i=1}^{n_{\rm te}}
with
n_{\rm te}
test samples following the data generation distribution in equation
1
and we define
\mathbf{X}=[{\bm{x}}_{\rm{te},1},\dots,{\bm{x}}_{\rm{te},n}]^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{\rm te}\times d}
. Let
\mathbf{F}_{\rm te}=\sigma({\mathbf{X}_{\rm te}\mathbf{W}^{\top}})\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{\rm te}\times N}
be the test feature matrix. We can write the test error as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}({\mathbf{F}}))=\lim_{n_{\rm te}\to\infty}\frac{1}{n_{\rm te}}\|{\bm{y}}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te}\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F})\|_{2}^{2}.

Now, consider the spiked approximation of the test feature matrix
\mathbf{F}_{\rm te,\ell}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{\rm te},N}
where each row of
\mathbf{F}_{\rm te,\ell}
follows the approximation in equation
41
. Using Theorem
3.1
, we have
\|\mathbf{F}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te,\ell}\|_{\rm op}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n_{\rm te}})
. Thus,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{n_{\rm te}}\|{\bm{y}}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te}\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F})\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n_{\rm te}}\|{\bm{y}}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te,\ell}\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F})\|_{2}^{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n_{\rm te}}\left|\Big\langle(\mathbf{F}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te,\ell})\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}),2{\bm{y}}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te,\ell}\Big\rangle\right|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n_{\rm te}}}\|\mathbf{F}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te,\ell}\|_{\rm op}\cdot\|\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F})\|_{2}\to_{\mathbb{P}}0,

where the last line is due to the fact that under the assumption of Theorem
4.2
, we have
\|{\bm{y}}_{\rm te}-\mathbf{F}_{\rm te}\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F})\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{n_{\rm te}})
, and that
\|\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F})\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
from the proof of Theorem
4.1
. Thus in the test error, we can replace the test features with their spiked approximation without changing the limiting test error. With this, we can write

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}+\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{k}c_{k}\eta^{k}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{k}\left({\bm{a}}^{\circ k}\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})^{\top}+\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}){\bm{a}}^{\circ k\top}\right)\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{i+j}\eta^{i+j}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{i+j}c_{i}c_{j}({\bm{a}}^{\circ i}{\bm{a}}^{\circ j\top})\right]

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left[\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{k}c_{k}\eta^{k}\left({\bm{a}}^{\circ k}\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}^{\top}+\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}{\bm{a}}^{\circ k\top}\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}\sum_{j=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{i+j}\eta^{i+j}c_{i}c_{j}\aleph_{i+j}({\bm{a}}^{\circ i}{\bm{a}}^{\circ j\top})\right],

(42)

where
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left[\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})^{\top}\right]
,
\boldsymbol{\nu}_{k}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}[({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{k}\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})]
, and
{\aleph_{k}}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{k}
for all
k\in[\ell]
.
Also,

\displaystyle{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te},y_{\rm te}}\left[\,y_{\rm te}\sigma(\mathbf{W}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})\,\right]={\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}^{0}+\sum_{k=1}^{\ell}c_{1}^{k}c_{k}\tau_{k}\eta^{k}{\bm{a}}^{\circ k},

(43)

where
{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}^{0}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te},y_{\rm te}}\left[\,y_{\rm te}\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})\,\right]
, and
\tau_{k}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left[y_{\rm te}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{k}\right]
.

M.1
Proof for
\ell=1

Without loss of generality, assume that
c_{1}=1
. First, note that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}[({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})]=\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta},

\displaystyle\aleph_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})=0,\quad\text{and}\quad\aleph_{2}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\to_{P}c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}),

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=\mathbb{E}\left[y_{\rm te}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})\right]=c_{\star,1}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}c_{\star,1}^{2},

where the convergence follows from equation
3
and the computations are in its proof.

Thus, using equation
M
, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}={\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}^{0}+c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta{\bm{a}},\quad\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\left(c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})\right){\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}.

From Section
J.1
, we have
\bar{\mathbf{R}}=\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
, where
\mathbf{T}
is defined in equation
27
and
\mathbf{U}=[\;\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}\;|\;\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\;]
. In Section
J.1
it was shown that

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}=\frac{\begin{bmatrix}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}&-\frac{1}{\eta}-{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}~\vskip 4.26773pt\\
-\frac{1}{\eta}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}&{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix}}{\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)\left({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}\right)-\left(\frac{1}{\eta}+{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)^{2}},

(44)

where
T_{11},T_{22}=\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and
T_{21},T_{12}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta)
.

Now, we are ready to study the terms
E_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te},y_{\rm te}}(y_{\rm te}^{2})
,
E_{2}=\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\hat{\bm{a}}
, and
E_{3}=-2\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}

that appear in the test error in equation
40
.

M.1.1
Analysis of
E_{1}
.

This term can be readily computed as

\displaystyle E_{1}=\mathbb{E}(y_{\rm te}^{2})=\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left(\sigma_{\star}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})\right)^{2}=\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+c_{\star}^{2}.

(45)

M.1.2
Analysis of
E_{2}
.

Recall that
\hat{\bm{a}}=(\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{F}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{y}}
,
\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}
, and
\bar{\mathbf{R}}=(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}
. Using these, we can write

\displaystyle E_{2}

\displaystyle=\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\hat{\bm{a}}=\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{F}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}(\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{F}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{y}}=\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\tilde{\bm{y}}.

(46)

We have
\tilde{\bm{y}}={\bm{f}}_{\star}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}
, thus

\displaystyle E_{2}={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+2\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}.

(47)

We will now analyze the terms in equation
47
. First note that
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
using a simple order-wise argument. To analyze the third term in equation
47
, we write

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{F}_{0}+\eta(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}){\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}+\eta(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}){\bm{a}}.

Using a simple order-wise analysis, we have
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
.
Thus, the third term can be written as

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}}_{q_{1}}+\underbrace{2\eta(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}{\bm{a}}}_{q_{2}}+\underbrace{\eta^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})^{2}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{a}}}_{q_{3}}.

The term
q_{1}
can be computed as

\displaystyle q_{1}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{F}^{\top}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}^{\top}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}^{\top}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where in the last line we have used
that
\bar{\mathbf{R}}=\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
and an order-wise analysis for various terms.
To analyze
q_{2}
, note that

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}{\bm{a}}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right){\bm{a}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

using a simple order-wise analysis, thus
q_{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Similarly, for
q_{3}
, we have

\displaystyle q_{3}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon})^{2}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right){\bm{a}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Hence, summing everything up

\displaystyle E_{2}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}^{\top}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}+{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(48)

Next, we will study the term
{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}
. We can write

\displaystyle\hat{\bm{a}}=\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{F}_{0}+\eta(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}){\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)^{\top}\left(\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\right){\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}}_{{\bm{p}}_{1}}\underbrace{-\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}}_{{\bm{p}}_{2}}+\underbrace{\eta\left[(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}-(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right]{\bm{a}}}_{{\bm{p}}_{3}}.

(49)

Thus, defining
E_{2}^{ij}={\bm{p}}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}{\bm{p}}_{j}
for
i,j\in[3]
, we have
E_{2}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{3}E_{2}^{ij}
. In the following sections, we will analyze each term in this sum separately.

Preliminary Computations.

Before starting the computation, we define
K
as

\displaystyle K:=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}-\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}.

(50)

Recalling that
T_{12}=T_{21}=O(1/\eta)
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}=O(1/\sqrt{n})
, the variable
K
can be simplified as follows:

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}-T_{22}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})+O_{\mathbb{P}}\left(\frac{1}{\eta\sqrt{n}}\right).

Also, from the definition of the matrix
\mathbf{T}
in equation
44
, we have

\displaystyle T_{22}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}}+\frac{1}{(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2})}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2}).

Hence, putting everything together, and using Lemma
J.4
, we can write

\displaystyle K

\displaystyle=-\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}}{(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2})}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2}).

(51)

Next, we will study the limit of
\eta T_{12}
. For this, we can use equation
44
to write

\displaystyle T_{12}=\frac{-1}{(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2})}\cdot\frac{1}{\eta}+O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{3}).

(52)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{11}
.

Noting that
\eta=o(n^{1/4})
and
{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
, we can simplify this term as follows:

\displaystyle E_{2}^{11}

\displaystyle={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(53)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{12}
and
E_{2}^{21}
.

By expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
and
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21}

\displaystyle=E_{2}^{12}=-{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=-{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left(\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=E_{2}^{21(1)}+E_{2}^{21(2)}+E_{2}^{21(3)}+E_{2}^{21(4)},

in which

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21(1)}

\displaystyle=-{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star},

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21(2)}

\displaystyle=-\eta\,({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}),

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21(3)}

\displaystyle=-\eta({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}),

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21(4)}

\displaystyle=-\eta^{2}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}).

These terms can be simplified as follows. By expanding
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21(1)}

\displaystyle=-{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=-{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\times

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\left[T_{11}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{12}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+T_{21}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{22}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\right]\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=-T_{22}\left({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right)+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1),

where the last line uses
that
{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
and
{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Next, noting that
\eta({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)
and
\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}=\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, we can write

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21(2)}=-\eta\,({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1).

Using a similar argument, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21(3)}

\displaystyle=-\eta({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})

\displaystyle=-\eta T_{12}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Finally, again note that
\eta^{2}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Thus,

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21(4)}=-\eta^{2}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})=o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1).

Putting all together, we arrive at

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21}=E_{2}^{12}

\displaystyle=-T_{22}\left({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)\left({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)
