\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\eta T_{12}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(54)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{22}
.

Once again, by expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
and
\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22}

\displaystyle={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\left[T_{11}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{12}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+T_{21}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{22}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\right]\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\left[\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt\times\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\left[T_{11}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{12}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+T_{21}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{22}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\right]\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}.

Note that
{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
and
\eta^{2}=o(\sqrt{n})
. Hence,

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22}

\displaystyle=({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}\left[T_{21}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{22}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left[\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right]\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}

(55)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\times\left[T_{12}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})+T_{22}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\right]+o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)

\displaystyle=({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}\Big[T_{22}^{2}\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 17.07182pt+2\eta T_{12}T_{22}({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})+\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\eta^{2}T_{12}^{2}({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{2}\Big]+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(56)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{13}
and
E_{2}^{31}
.

Recalling the definition of
K
in equation
50
, this term can be written as

\displaystyle E_{2}^{13}

\displaystyle=E_{2}^{31}=\eta K{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{a}}

\displaystyle=\eta K{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left[\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right]{\bm{a}}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}K({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(57)

where the last line uses equation
51
and that
\eta\,{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Analysis of
E_{2}^{23}
and
E_{2}^{32}
.

Again, recalling the definition of
K
in equation
50
, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23}

\displaystyle=E_{2}^{32}=-\eta K\;{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{a}}

\displaystyle=-\eta K\;{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\left[T_{11}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{12}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+T_{21}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{22}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\times\left[\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}{\bm{a}}+\eta\left(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{a}})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}+\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}\right]

\displaystyle=E_{2}^{23(1)}+E_{2}^{23(2)}+E_{2}^{23(3)}+E_{2}^{23(4)},

in which each term can be written as follows:

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23(1)}

\displaystyle=-\eta KT_{11}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\Big[{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}{\bm{a}}+\eta({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\eta({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\Big]=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where we have used that
K=\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2})
,
{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and
\eta\,{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{a}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Also,

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23(2)}

\displaystyle=-\eta KT_{12}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\Big[\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}{\bm{a}}+\eta({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\eta(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\Big]=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

with a very similar argument to that for
E_{2}^{23(1)}
. Next,

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23(3)}

\displaystyle=-\eta KT_{21}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\Big[{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}{\bm{a}}+\eta({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})

\displaystyle\hskip 128.0374pt+\eta({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\Big]

\displaystyle=-\eta^{3}KT_{21}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

in which we have used the fact that
T_{21}=\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta)
, and
K=\Theta(1/\eta^{2})
. Finally, with a similar argument

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23(4)}

\displaystyle=-\eta KT_{22}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\Big[\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}{\bm{a}}+\eta({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})

\displaystyle\hskip 122.34692pt+\eta(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\Big]

\displaystyle=-\eta^{2}KT_{22}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Putting everything together, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23}=-\eta^{3}KT_{21}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})

\displaystyle-\eta^{2}KT_{22}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(58)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{33}
.

This term can be analyzed by expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
as follows:

\displaystyle E_{2}^{33}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}K^{2}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\left[\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\right]{\bm{a}}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}K^{2}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}{\bm{a}}+2\eta^{3}K^{2}({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+\eta^{4}K^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}=\eta^{4}K^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(59)

where we have used that
K=\Theta_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2})
and
\eta\,{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Putting Everything Together.

Now, we can put
together
the results from previous sections to derive the limiting value of
E_{2}
. First, we will explicitly derive the limit of each component.
To do so, recall that using equation
3
and Lemma
J.4
, we have

\displaystyle\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\to_{P}\left[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}\right],\quad{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}c_{\star,1}^{2}\psi m_{2}/\phi

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}\left[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}\right]\psi m_{2}/{\phi},\quad\text{and}\quad{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}\to_{P}\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}.

Also, using Lemma
J.1
, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}c_{\star,1}^{2}M,\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}\left[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}\right]M,

in which

M:=\lim_{n,N,d\to\infty}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}
.
This limit has
been computed in
[
AP20a
]
. Using the diagram in the proof of Lemma
J.4
that shows how the notations of
[
AP20a
]
match ours, we find that

E_{32}
in
[
AP20a
, S148]
equals our
M
.
Thus, we find

\displaystyle M=1-\frac{2m_{2}}{m1}-\frac{m_{2}^{\prime}}{m_{1}^{2}},

(60)

where
m_{2}^{\prime}
is the derivative of
m_{2}
with respect to
\lambda
.
Also, again using
J.1
, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}c_{\star,1}^{2}\bar{M},\quad\text{and}\quad\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}\left[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}\right]\bar{M},

in which

\bar{M}:=\lim_{n,N,d\to\infty}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}_{\star}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}
.
This limit has been computed in
[
BES+22
]
.
Specifically, using
T_{9}^{0}
in (C.16), and noting that their
\mathbf{\Phi}_{0}
translates to
\mathbf{F}_{0}/\sqrt{N}
,
their
\mathbf{W}_{0}
translates to
\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}
, and their
\tilde{\lambda}
translates to
\frac{\lambda n}{N}
in our notation, using
[
BES+22
, Proposition 29]
we find

\displaystyle\bar{M}=1-\frac{m_{2}}{m_{1}}.

(61)

Now, we can use equation
M.1.2
, equation
M.1.2
, equation
M.1.2
, equation
M.1.2
, and equation
M.1.2
,
respectively, to derive the following expressions:

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21}=E_{2}^{12}=\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[-M+\bar{M}\left(\frac{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1}\right)\right],

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22}=\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[M-2\bar{M}\left(\frac{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1}\right)+\left(\frac{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1}\right)^{2}\right],

\displaystyle E_{2}^{13}=E_{2}^{31}=-\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[\frac{\bar{M}}{\psi/\phi+\lambda\psi^{2}/\phi^{2}-1}\right],

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23}=E_{2}^{32}=\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[-\frac{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}}{(\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1)^{2}}+\frac{\bar{M}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1}\right],

\displaystyle E_{2}^{33}=\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{(\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1)^{2}}\right].

Thus, summing these terms up, we conclude that

\displaystyle E_{2}-\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}\left(1-M\right)}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}},

(62)

wrapping up the derivation of the limiting value of
E_{2}
.

M.1.3
Analysis of
E_{3}
.

To analyze this term, first note that

\displaystyle E_{3}

\displaystyle=-2\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=-2\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=-2{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}-2\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}.

With a simple order-wise analysis, the second term can be shown to be
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).
To analyze the first term, we will again use the decomposition from equation
M.1.2
.
We can write

-2{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=E_{3}^{(1)}+E_{3}^{(2)}+E_{3}^{(3)}
,
in which
E_{3}^{(i)}=-2{\bm{p}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}
. We will analyze these terms separately. For the first term, we have

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(1)}

\displaystyle=-2\,{\bm{p}}_{1}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=-2\,{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left(c_{\star,1}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta\,{\bm{a}}\right)=-2c_{\star,1}{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

where we have used that
\eta\,{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Similarly, the second term can be written as

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=-2\,{\bm{p}}_{2}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=2\,{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left(c_{\star,1}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta\,{\bm{a}}\right)

\displaystyle=2\,{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\left[T_{11}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{12}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+T_{21}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{22}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left(c_{\star,1}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta\,{\bm{a}}\right)

\displaystyle=2\,({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\left[T_{21}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{22}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\right]\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\left(c_{\star,1}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta\,{\bm{a}}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=2\,({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\left[c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta T_{21}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}+c_{\star,1}T_{22}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\right]+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Also, for the third term we can write

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(3)}

\displaystyle=-2\,{\bm{p}}_{3}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=-2\eta K{\bm{a}}^{\top}\left(c_{\star,1}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta\,{\bm{a}}\right)=-2c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta^{2}K+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Summing up, the limiting value for
E_{3}
is

\displaystyle E_{3}+2c_{\star,1}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[2\bar{M}-2\right].

(63)

M.1.4
The Final Result

Putting equations
45
,
62
,
63
together, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}

\displaystyle(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}))-\Big(\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+c_{\star}^{2}+\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}-2c_{\star,1}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\Big)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[2\bar{M}-1-M\right].

The test error of the untrained random features model can be written as

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}_{0}))=\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+c_{\star}^{2}

\displaystyle+\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}-2c_{\star,1}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}.

Hence, the improvement over the untrained random features model in terms of test error is equal to

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}_{0}))-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}))\to_{P}

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[1+M-2\bar{M}\right].

Hence, using equation
61
and equation
60
, we find

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}_{0}))-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}))\to_{P}\frac{-c_{\star,1}^{4}}{(\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2})m_{1}^{2}}\frac{\partial m_{2}}{\partial\lambda},

(64)

where
\frac{\partial m_{1}}{\partial\lambda}\leq 0
using that
\operatorname{tr}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}})/d=\psi m_{2}/\phi
. This concludes the proof for
\ell=1
.

M.2
Proof for
\ell=2

First,
similar to the proof for
\ell=1
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\nu}_{2}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}[({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}\sigma(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})]=2c_{2}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2},

\displaystyle\aleph_{3}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{3}=0,\text{ and }\quad\aleph_{4}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{4}=3\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\to_{P}3[c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})],

\displaystyle\tau_{2}=\mathbb{E}\left[y_{\rm te}({\bm{x}}_{\rm te}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}\right]=2c_{\star,2}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}\to_{P}2c_{\star,1}^{2}c_{\star,2},

in which we have used Lemma
C.1
. Thus, using equation
M
and equation
43
, we have

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}

\displaystyle=\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}+\eta\left({\bm{a}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}){\bm{a}}^{\top}\right)+\frac{2c_{2}^{2}\eta^{2}}{\sqrt{N}}\left((\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}+(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top})\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\eta^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}{\bm{a}}^{\top}+\frac{3c_{2}^{2}\eta^{4}}{N}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{4}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}),\quad\text{and}\quad

\displaystyle{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}

\displaystyle={\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}^{0}+c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta{\bm{a}}+2c_{2}c_{\star,1}^{2}c_{\star,2}\eta^{2}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}.

Also, from Section
J.2
, we have
\bar{\mathbf{R}}=\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
, where the matrix
\mathbf{T}^{-1}
is defined in equation
32
and
\mathbf{U}=[\,\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}\;|\;\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\sqrt{N}\;|\;\;\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\;|\;(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\;]
. Using the analysis in Section
J.2.1
, we note that

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}^{-1}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}&0&\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}&0\\
0&{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}&0&\frac{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}{c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\eta^{2}}\\
\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2}\eta}&0&(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})&0\\[14.22636pt]
0&\frac{N^{\frac{1}{2}}}{c_{1}^{2}c_{2}\eta^{2}}&0&(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\end{bmatrix}+\mathbf{\Delta},

in which the elements of
\mathbf{\Delta}
are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
. Hence, the matrix
\mathbf{T}=[T_{i,j}]
has entries

\displaystyle T_{11}=\frac{1}{{\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{({\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}})^{2}((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}))}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2}),

\displaystyle T_{13}=T_{31}=-\frac{1}{\eta}\cdot\frac{1}{({\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}))}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta),

\displaystyle T_{22}=\frac{1}{{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}}+\frac{N}{c_{2}^{2}\eta^{4}}\cdot\frac{1}{({N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{2}((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(N/\eta^{4}),

\displaystyle T_{24}=T_{42}=-\frac{\sqrt{N}}{c_{2}\eta^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{({N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2}),

\displaystyle T_{33}=\frac{1}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})}+\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{({\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}))^{2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2}),

\displaystyle T_{44}=\frac{1}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}}+\frac{N}{c_{2}^{2}\eta^{4}}\cdot\frac{1}{(N{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2})^{2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(N/\eta^{4}),

and its other elements are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
.

Next, we will study the terms
E_{1}=\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te},y_{\rm te}}(y_{\rm te}^{2})
,
E_{2}=\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\hat{\bm{a}}
, and
E_{3}=-2\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}

that appear in the decomposition of the test error in equation
40
.

M.2.1
Analysis of
E_{1}

Similar to the
\ell=1
case, we have

\displaystyle E_{1}=\mathbb{E}(y_{\rm te}^{2})=\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{{\bm{x}}_{\rm te}}\left(\sigma_{\star}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{\rm te})\right)^{2}=\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}+c_{\star}^{2}.

(65)

M.2.2
Analysis of
E_{2}

Recall that using equation
47
, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+2\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\tilde{\bm{f}}_{\star}+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}.

First, note that
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
using a simple order-wise argument.
Using an argument similar to the one for
\ell=1
,
the third term can be written as
\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}=\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\,\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Next, we will study the term
{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}
. We can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\left(\mathbf{F}_{0}+\eta(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}){\bm{a}}^{\top}+c_{2}\eta^{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\right)^{\top}\left(\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\right){\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\underbrace{\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}}_{{\bm{p}}_{1}}\underbrace{-\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}}_{{\bm{p}}_{2}}+\underbrace{\eta K_{1}{\bm{a}}}_{{\bm{p}}_{3}}+\underbrace{c_{2}\eta^{2}K_{2}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}}_{{\bm{p}}_{4}},

(66)

in which
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
are defined as

\displaystyle K_{1}=\left[(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}-(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right],

\displaystyle K_{2}=\left[(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}-(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right].

Usign this notation, we have

{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}=\sum_{i,j\in[4]}E_{2}^{ij}
,
where
E_{2}^{ij}={\bm{p}}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{p}}_{j}^{\top}
. In the following sections, we will compute each term separately.

Preliminary Computations.

First, we will analyze
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
. Recall that

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}=T_{11}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})

\displaystyle(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{13}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}

\displaystyle+NT_{22}\left(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\right)\left(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\right)^{\top}+\sqrt{N}T_{24}\left(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\right)(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}

\displaystyle+T_{31}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{\top}+T_{33}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}+\sqrt{N}T_{42}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}(\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top})

\displaystyle+T_{44}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=T_{33}\left[(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\right]\cdot\left[(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right]+O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n}),

which gives

\displaystyle K_{1}=-\frac{1}{\eta^{2}}\cdot\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{f}}_{\star}}{({\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}))}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2}).

(67)

Similarly, for
K_{2}
we have

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=T_{44}\left[(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\right]\cdot\left[(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right]+O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n}),

which gives

\displaystyle K_{2}=-\frac{N}{c_{2}^{2}\eta^{4}}\cdot\frac{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{f}}_{\star}}{(N{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\mathbf{I}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2})}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{4}).

(68)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{11}
.

For this term, by expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
, we can write

\displaystyle E_{2}^{11}

\displaystyle={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(69)

This holds because
\eta{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and
\frac{\eta^{2}}{\sqrt{N}}{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Analysis of
E_{2}^{12}
and
E_{2}^{21}
.

We have

E_{2}^{21}=E_{2}^{12}=-{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}
.
Using the expression for
\mathbf{T}
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}=\begin{bmatrix}T_{11}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+T_{13}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\\[5.69046pt]
T_{22}\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+T_{24}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\\[5.69046pt]
T_{31}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+T_{33}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\\[5.69046pt]
T_{42}\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+T_{44}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\end{bmatrix}.

(70)

On the other hand, by expanding
\mathbf{U}
and
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
, we similarly have

\displaystyle({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U})=\begin{bmatrix}\eta({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\\[5.69046pt]
\frac{2c_{2}^{2}\eta^{2}}{\sqrt{N}}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2})((\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}))\\[5.69046pt]
{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}\\[5.69046pt]
{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\end{bmatrix}^{\top}.

(71)

This gives

\displaystyle E_{2}^{21}=E_{2}^{12}=

\displaystyle-T_{13}\eta({\bm{f}}_{\star}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})

\displaystyle-\frac{2c_{2}^{2}\eta^{2}T_{24}}{\sqrt{N}}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2})((\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}))((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})

\displaystyle-T_{33}({\bm{f}}_{\star}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})

\displaystyle-T_{44}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2})((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(72)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{13}
and
E_{2}^{31}
.

Recalling equation
67
, by expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{13}=E_{2}^{31}

\displaystyle=\eta K_{1}{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{a}}=\eta^{2}K_{1}({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(73)

in which we have used that
K_{1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2})
.

Analysis of
E_{2}^{14}
and
E_{2}^{41}
.

Recalling equation
68
, by expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{14}=E_{2}^{41}

\displaystyle=c_{2}\eta^{2}K_{2}{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(74)

in which we have used that
\eta^{4}K_{2}/N=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Analysis of
E_{2}^{22}
.

This term is equal to

E_{2}^{22}={\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}
.
Using equation
70
, we can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=[T_{11}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+T_{13}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}]\;\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+[T_{22}\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+T_{24}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}]\;\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+[T_{31}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+T_{33}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}]\;\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+[T_{42}\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}+T_{44}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}]\;\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}.

(75)

Further, by expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22}=E_{2}^{22(1)}+E_{2}^{22(2)}+E_{2}^{22(3)}+E_{2}^{22(4)}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(76)

in which

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22(1)}

\displaystyle=[T_{33}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}]^{2}\;(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+[T_{44}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}]^{2}\;((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}),

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22(2)}

\displaystyle=2\eta T_{13}T_{33}\cdot((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})^{2}({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}),

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22(3)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4c_{2}^{2}\eta^{2}}{\sqrt{N}}T_{24}T_{44}((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})^{2}\left((\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})\right)\left((\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\right),

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22(4)}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}T_{13}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\;((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})^{2}\,({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})^{2}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{3c_{2}^{2}\eta^{4}}{N}T_{24}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{4}\left((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right)^{2}\,\left((\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})\right)^{2}.

Analysis of
E_{2}^{23}
and
E_{2}^{32}
.

We have

E_{2}^{23}=E_{2}^{32}=-\eta K_{1}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}
.
Recall that the vector
\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}
has been computed in equation
M.2.2
.
With this, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23}=E_{2}^{32}=

\displaystyle-\eta^{2}K_{1}T_{33}(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})

\displaystyle-\eta^{3}K_{1}T_{13}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})(\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(77)

in which we used that
\eta^{2}K_{1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and
\eta T_{13}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

Analysis of
E_{2}^{24}
and
E_{2}^{42}
.

This term can be written as

\displaystyle E_{24}=E_{42}=-c_{2}\eta^{2}K_{2}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}.

The vector
\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}
has been computed in equation
M.2.2
. By expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{24}=E_{2}^{42}=-\frac{2c_{2}^{3}\eta^{4}K_{2}T_{44}}{N}\cdot\left((\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\right)\cdot\left((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-\frac{3c_{2}^{3}\eta^{6}K_{2}T_{24}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{4}}{N^{3/2}}\left((\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})\right)\cdot\left((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Now, noting that
K_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N/\eta^{4})
from equation
68
and
\|(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{N})
, we find that the first term is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
and we have

\displaystyle E_{2}^{24}=E_{2}^{42}=-\frac{3c_{2}^{3}\eta^{6}K_{2}T_{24}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{4}}{N^{3/2}}\left((\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})\right)\cdot\left((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(78)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{33}
.

Similar to the
\ell=1
case, this term can be written as

\displaystyle E_{2}^{33}

\displaystyle=\eta^{2}K_{1}^{2}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{a}}=\eta^{4}K_{1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

(79)

noting that
K_{1}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\eta^{2})
.

Analysis of
E_{2}^{34}
and
E_{2}^{43}
.

By expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
, we readily arrive at

\displaystyle E_{2}^{34}=E_{2}^{43}=\eta^{3}K_{1}K_{2}c_{2}{\bm{a}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(80)

Analysis of
E_{2}^{44}
.

This term can be written as

E_{2}^{44}=c_{2}^{2}\eta^{4}K_{2}^{2}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}
.
By expanding
\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}
and noting that
K_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(N/\eta^{4})
, we can write

\displaystyle E_{2}^{44}=\frac{3c_{2}^{4}\eta^{8}K_{2}^{2}}{N^{2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(81)

Putting Everything Together.

Now, we can use the results derived above to compute the limiting value of
E_{2}
. Recall that from Lemma
J.7
and Lemma
J.8
we have

\displaystyle H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\to_{P}2c_{\star,1}^{2}\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi},\quad\text{and}\quad(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\to_{P}\frac{3\psi m_{1}}{\phi}[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}.

Also using an argument similar to the argument in the proof of Lemma
J.7
and Lemma
J.8
, we have

\displaystyle H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\to_{p}2c_{\star,1}^{2}\hat{M},

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2}\to 3\hat{M}[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2},

in which

\hat{M}=\lim_{n,N,d\to\infty}\frac{1}{n}\operatorname{tr}\left[\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\right]
.
This term has been computed in
[
AP20a
]
.
Using the diagram in the proof of Lemma
J.4
that shows how the notations of
[
AP20a
]
match ours, we find that we can use (S142) with
\sigma_{\varepsilon}=0
in
[
AP20a
]
, to find

\displaystyle\hat{M}=-\frac{m_{1}^{\prime}}{m_{1}^{2}}-1,

(82)

where
m_{1}^{\prime}
is the derivative of
m_{1}
with respect to
\lambda
.

For brevity, we will define

\tilde{A}:=\lim_{n,N,d\to\infty}\left[(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2\top})\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})\right]
.
With this, equations
M.2.2
,
73
,
74
,
76
,
M.2.2
,
78
,
79
,
80
, and
81
give

\displaystyle E_{2}^{12}=E_{2}^{21}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[\bar{M}\left(\frac{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1}\right)-M\right]-\frac{4c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}\hat{M}}{3[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}},

\displaystyle E_{2}^{13}=E_{2}^{31}\to_{P}-\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\bar{M}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1},\quad E_{2}^{14}=E_{2}^{41}\to_{P}0,

as well as

\displaystyle E_{2}^{22}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[M-2\bar{M}\left(\frac{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1}\right)+\left(\frac{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}}{[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}}\left[4{\hat{M}/3}+\frac{4\tilde{A}^{2}}{3(\tilde{A}-1)^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle E_{2}^{23}=E_{2}^{32}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[\frac{\bar{M}}{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1}-\frac{\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}}{(\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1)^{2}}\right],

and also

\displaystyle E_{2}^{24}=E_{2}^{42}\to_{P}-\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}}{[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}}\left[\frac{4\tilde{A}}{3(\tilde{A}-1)^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle E_{2}^{33}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left[\frac{1}{(\psi/\phi-\lambda\psi^{2}m_{1}/\phi^{2}-1)^{2}}\right],

\displaystyle E_{2}^{34}=E_{2}^{43}\to_{P}0,\quad E_{2}^{44}=E_{2}^{44}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}}{[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}}\left[\frac{4}{3(\tilde{A}-1)^{2}}\right],

respectively. Putting these together, the component
E_{2}
can be written as

\displaystyle E_{2}-\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}\boldsymbol{\Sigma}_{{\bm{f}}}^{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{y}}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}(1-M)}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}+\frac{4c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}(1-\hat{M})}{3[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}}.

(83)

M.2.3
Analysis of
E_{3}
.

To analyze this component, first note that

\displaystyle E_{3}

\displaystyle=-2\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=-2\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}=-2{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}-2\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}\mathbf{F}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}.

Using a simple order-wise analysis, it can be shown that the second term is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Now, recalling equation
M.2.2
we write
E_{3}=\sum_{i=1}^{4}E_{3}^{(i)}
, where
E_{3}^{(i)}=-2{\bm{p}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}
. By expanding
{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}
and recalling that
{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}^{0}=c_{\star,1}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}
, we have

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(1)}=-2{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}^{0}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(84)

Next, recall that the matrix
\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{U}\mathbf{T}\mathbf{U}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}
is analyzed in equation
M.2.2
. Using this, and by expanding
{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}
, we get

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(2)}

\displaystyle=2({\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})\left[T_{33}({\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}^{0\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})+c_{\star,1}^{2}T_{13}\eta({\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}})\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 36.98866pt+\frac{4c_{2}c_{\star,1}^{2}c_{\star,2}T_{24}\eta^{2}}{\sqrt{N}}\left[(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})\right]\left((\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{f}}_{\star}\right)+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

(85)

Similarly, by expanding
{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}
, we arrive at

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(3)}=-2c_{\star,1}^{2}\eta^{2}K_{1}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),\quad\text{and}\quad E_{3}^{(4)}=-\frac{4c_{2}^{2}c_{\star,1}^{2}c_{\star,2}\eta^{4}K_{2}}{N}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Similar to the computation for
E_{2}
, we can derive the limiting values of the components in
E_{3}
as

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(2)}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left(2\bar{M}-\frac{2\tilde{A}}{\tilde{A}-1}\right)-\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}}{[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}}\left(\frac{8\tilde{A}}{3(\tilde{A}-1)}\right),

\displaystyle E_{3}^{(3)}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}\left(\frac{2}{\tilde{A}-1}\right),\quad E_{3}^{(4)}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}}{[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}}\left(\frac{8}{3(\tilde{A}-1)}\right).

Putting these together, we have

\displaystyle E_{3}+2{\bm{f}}_{\star}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{\mu}}_{\bm{f}}^{0}\to_{P}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}\left(2\bar{M}-2\right)}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}-\frac{8c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}}{3[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}}.

(86)

M.2.4
The Final Result

Putting equations
65
,
83
, and
86
, we have

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}_{0}))-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}))\to_{P}

\displaystyle\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}(1+M-2\bar{M})}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}+\frac{4c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}(1+\hat{M})}{3[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}},

where
{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}_{0}))
is the test error of the untrained random feature model. Further, using equation
60
, equation
61
, and equation
82
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}_{0}))-{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm te}(\hat{\bm{a}}(\mathbf{F}))\to_{P}

\displaystyle-\,\frac{c_{\star,1}^{4}}{(\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2})m_{1}^{2}}\frac{\partial m_{2}}{\partial\lambda}-\frac{4c_{\star,1}^{4}c_{\star,2}^{2}}{3[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}m_{1}^{2}}\frac{\partial m_{1}}{\partial\lambda}.

(87)

Note that
\frac{\partial m_{1}}{\partial\lambda},\frac{\partial m_{2}}{\partial\lambda}\leq 0
, concluding the proof.

Appendix N
Proofs of Supplementary Lemmas

N.1
Proof of Lemma
C.3

Recalling
a_{i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}{\sf N}(0,1/N)
and
\langle\tilde{\bm{x}}_{i},\boldsymbol{\beta}\rangle|\boldsymbol{\beta}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}{\sf N}(0,\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2})
, claims (a) and (b) follow from standard Gaussian maximal inequalities
[
vdVW13
, Section 2.2]

and from
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
; the latter follows
by writing

\boldsymbol{\beta}=n^{-1}\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\sigma_{\star}(\mathbf{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})+\varepsilon)
,
where
\varepsilon=(\varepsilon_{1},\ldots,\varepsilon_{n})^{\top}
and using our distributional assumptions on
\mathbf{X},\varepsilon
,
as well as Condition
2.2
.

By
[
Ver12
, Theorem 5.39]
and
[
BS10
, Corollary A.21]
, we have
\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\|_{\textnormal{op}},\|(\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top})^{\circ 2}\|_{\textnormal{op}}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Also, by
[
Ver18
, Theorem 3.4.6]
and Gaussian maximal inequalities
[
vdVW13
, Section 2.2]
, we have
\max_{1\leq i\neq\leq j\leq N}\langle{\bm{w}}_{0,i},{\bm{w}}_{0,j}\rangle=O_{\mathbb{P}}(n^{-\frac{1}{2}}\log^{\frac{1}{2}}n)
.
For
k\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\|(\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top})^{\circ k}\|_{\textnormal{op}}

\displaystyle\leq\|(\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top})^{\circ k}-\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\textnormal{op}}+1\leq\|(\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top})^{\circ k}-\mathbf{I}_{N}\|_{\textnormal{F}}+1

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sum_{1\leq i\neq j\leq N}\langle{\bm{w}}_{0,i},{\bm{w}}_{0,j}\rangle^{2k}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+1=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)+1.

Therefore,

\displaystyle M_{W_{0}}\leq\max\left\{\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\|_{\textnormal{op}},\|(\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top})^{\circ 2}\|_{\textnormal{op}},\sup_{k\geq 3}\|(\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top})^{\circ k}\|_{\textnormal{op}}\right\}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Claim (d) is standard, see e.g.
[
Ver18
, Theorem 4.4.5]
.

N.2
Proof of Lemma
J.1

We can write

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{\top}(\boldsymbol{\beta}-c_{\star,1}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\mathbf{v}^{\top}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}(\sigma_{\star}(\mathbf{X}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})+\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}))-c_{\star,1}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}

\displaystyle=n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\mathbf{v}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{i}\sigma_{\star}({\bm{x}}_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})-c_{\star,1}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})+n^{-1}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}.

Now
n^{-1}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\varepsilon}\sim{\sf N}(0,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2}\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2})/n\to_{P}0
.
Moreover,
by Condition
2.4
,
we can write

\sigma_{\star}({\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})=c_{\star,0}+c_{\star,1}{\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+(P_{>1}\sigma_{\star})({\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})
,
where
conditional on
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}
,

(P_{>1}\sigma_{\star})({\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})

is orthogonal in
L^{2}
to the constant function and to
{\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}
.
Hence the first sum above equals

\displaystyle n^{-1}c_{\star,0}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{x}}_{i}+n^{-1}c_{\star,1}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\biggl(\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{x}}_{i}{\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}-\mathbf{I}\biggr)\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}+n^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\mathbf{v}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{i}(P_{>1}\sigma_{\star})({\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}).

For the first term,

n^{-1}c_{\star,0}\mathbf{v}^{\top}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\bm{x}}_{i}\sim n^{-1}c_{\star,0}\cdot{\sf N}(0,n\|\mathbf{v}\|_{2}^{2})\to_{P}0
.
The
second term is
c_{\star,1}
times a sample mean of i.i.d. random variables of the form

\mathbf{v}^{\top}({\bm{x}}_{i}{\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}-1)\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}
,
which have zero mean by the Gaussianity of
{\bm{x}}_{i}
,
and for which all moments are finite.
Hence, by the weak law of large numbers, this term converges to zero in probability.

Similarly, the
third term
is a sample mean of i.i.d. random variables of the form

\mathbf{v}^{\top}{\bm{x}}_{i}(P_{>1}\sigma_{\star})({\bm{x}}^{\top}_{i}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})
,
which have zero mean by the Gaussianity of
{\bm{x}}_{i}
and Lemma
C.1
,
and whose second moments are finite since
\sigma_{\star}
is Lipschitz.
Hence, by the weak law of large numbers, this term also converges to zero in probability. This finishes the proof of the first claim.

Next, the second statement
follows from
[
BES+22
, Lemma 18]
.
While that work
has slightly different assumptions on the teacher function
f_{\star}
,
it is straightforward to check that their proof goes through unchanged under our assumptions.
Specifically, their proof requires that
{\bm{x}}\mapsto f_{\star}({\bm{x}})=\sigma_{\star}({\bm{x}}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})

is
O(1)
-Lipschitz, which holds in our case because
\sigma_{\star}
is

O(1)
-Lipschitz,
and
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

N.3
Proof of Lemma
J.2

By plugging in
\hat{\bm{a}}
into the training loss, we find

\displaystyle{\mathcal{L}}_{\rm tr}(\mathbf{F})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}-\mathbf{F}\hat{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}+\lambda\|\hat{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{n}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{2}{n}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\hat{\bm{a}}+\frac{1}{n}\hat{\bm{a}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{F}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})\hat{\bm{a}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\hat{\bm{a}}=\frac{1}{n}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{F}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\tilde{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\|\tilde{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}-\frac{1}{n}\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{y}}+\lambda\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{y}}

\displaystyle=\lambda\tilde{\bm{y}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}\mathbf{F}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\tilde{\bm{y}},

which proves the lemma.

N.4
Proof of Lemma
J.3

To prove the concentration of this term around its mean, we will use the generalized Hanson-Wright inequality
[
Sam23
, Theorem 2.1]
for
\alpha
-subexponential random variables. Note that, by definition, if
Z
is a Gaussian random variable,
H_{p}(Z)
is
2/p
-subexponential (see the definition in equation (1.1) of
[
Sam23
]
and for these variables the Orlicz norm of order
2/p
is bounded (see equation (1.3) of
[
Sam23
]
). Also note that
\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\rm Fr}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
. Thus, using
[
Sam23
, Theorem 2.1]
and setting
t=\frac{\log(n)}{\sqrt{n}}
, we find

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}\left(\Big|g(\mathbf{Z})^{\top}\mathbf{D}\;g(\mathbf{Z})-\mathbb{E}[g(\mathbf{Z})^{\top}\mathbf{D}\;g(\mathbf{Z})]\Big|\geq\frac{\log n}{\sqrt{n}}\right)\leq 2\exp\left(-C\min\left\{\log^{2}(n),(\sqrt{n}\log n)^{1/p}\right\}\right),

where
C>0
is some constant. This concludes the proof.

N.5
Proof of Lemma
J.4

First, we show that switching from
{\bm{w}}_{0,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}\mathrm{Unif}(\mathbb{S}^{d-1})
to
\hat{\bm{w}}_{0,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d})
will
not change the limit of
the terms
\frac{1}{d}\mathbb{E}\mathrm{tr}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}})
and
\mathbb{E}\mathrm{tr}(\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0})
which will appear later in the proof.
First, we define
\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}=[\hat{\bm{w}}_{0,1},\cdots,\hat{\bm{w}}_{0,N}]^{\top}
,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\text{diag}\left(\frac{1}{{\|\hat{\bm{w}}_{0,1}\|_{2}}},\cdots,\frac{1}{{\|\hat{\bm{w}}_{0,N}\|_{2}}}\right),\,\mathbf{W}_{0}=_{d}\mathbf{D}\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0},\,\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=\sigma(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{\top}),

\displaystyle\,\text{and}\qquad\hat{\bar{\mathbf{R}}}_{0}=(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}.

Then,

\displaystyle\biggl|\operatorname{tr}\left[\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\hat{\bar{\mathbf{R}}}_{0}\right]\biggr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\operatorname{tr}\left[(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}-(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\right]\biggr|

\displaystyle=\biggl|\operatorname{tr}\left[(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}-\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top})(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\right]\biggr|

\displaystyle\leq\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\|(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\|_{\rm op}\|\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\|\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}-\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}.

Now, using the Gaussian equivalence from Appendix
I
, we
can replace
\mathbf{F}_{0}
and
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}

with

\mathbf{F}_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
and
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
, respectively, without changing the limit.
With this, we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}-\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}=c_{1}^{2}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}-\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{\top})\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}+c_{1}c_{>1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}-\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0})^{\top}\mathbf{Z}^{\top}+c_{1}c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}(\mathbf{W}_{0}-\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0})\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}.

Now,

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}-\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}\leq\|\mathbf{I}_{N}-\mathbf{D}\|_{\rm op}\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}(\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\rm op}+1).

Note that
\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
\|\mathbf{D}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
, and
\|\mathbf{I}_{N}-\mathbf{D}\|_{\rm op}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Thus
\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}-\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Also, similarly,
\|\mathbf{W}_{0}-\hat{\mathbf{W}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Hence, noting that
\|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm op}
and
\|\mathbf{Z}\|_{\rm op}
are both
O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\|\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}-\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}\to_{P}0
. This implies that
|\operatorname{tr}[\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\hat{\bar{\mathbf{R}}}_{0}]|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Also,

\displaystyle\left|\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{tr}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]-\frac{1}{d}\operatorname{tr}\left[\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\hat{\bar{\mathbf{R}}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\right]\right|\leq|\operatorname{tr}[\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\hat{\bar{\mathbf{R}}}_{0}]|\frac{\|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\|_{\rm op}}{d}\to_{P}0.

Finally, we can prove the required claims as follows:

(a)

Since
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d})
, we have

\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}=\frac{1}{d}\,\mathbb{E}\mathrm{tr}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
,
by the Hanson-Wright inequality. Note that by the argument above, we can assume that
\hat{\bm{w}}_{0,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d})
without changing the limiting trace.
Further, from
[
AP20a
, Proposition 1]
, see also
[
ALP22
]
,
we have

\frac{1}{d}\,\mathbb{E}\mathrm{tr}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}})\to\frac{\psi}{\phi}m_{2}
; see the discussion at the end of this proof for the detailed explanation.
Now, we arrive at the conclusion by applying Lemma
J.1
.

(b)

Since
{\bm{a}}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{N}\mathbf{I}_{N})
, we have

{\bm{a}}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}-\|{\bm{a}}\|_{2}^{2}=\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\right)-1+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

by the Hanson-Wright inequality. Moreover,

\displaystyle\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}=\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N}-\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}=\mathbf{I}_{N}-\lambda n(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}.

Hence,

\frac{1}{N}\operatorname{tr}\left(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}\right)-1=-\frac{\lambda n}{N}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}
. From the argument above, we can assume that
\hat{\bm{w}}_{0,i}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle i.i.d.}}{{\sim}}{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d})
without changing the limiting trace.
It follows from
[
AP20a
, Proposition 1]
that
\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\to\frac{\psi}{\phi}m_{1}
;
again see the discussion at the end of this proof for the detailed explanation.
Note that
\lim\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
is the limiting Stieltjes transform of
\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}
. Hence,
\bar{m}_{1}=\lim\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}\mathbf{F}_{0}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{N})^{-1}
is the limiting companion Stieltjes transform of
m_{1}
which is given by

\bar{m}_{1}=\frac{\psi}{\phi}m_{1}-\left(1-\frac{\phi}{\psi}\right)\frac{1}{\lambda}.

(88)

This concludes the proof.

For the reader’s convenience, we provide the following diagram that shows how the notations of
[
AP20a
]
(left)
match (
\Leftrightarrow
)
ours (right):

\displaystyle n_{0}\Leftrightarrow d,\qquad n_{1}\Leftrightarrow N,\qquad m\Leftrightarrow n,\qquad\phi,\psi\Leftrightarrow\phi,\psi,

\displaystyle\mathbf{X}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n_{0}}\Leftrightarrow\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d},\qquad\mathbf{F}^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times n_{1}}\Leftrightarrow\mathbf{F}_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times N},\qquad\sigma_{W_{2}}=0,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n_{1}}\mathbf{K}(\lambda m/n_{1})^{-1}=\frac{1}{n_{1}}\mathbf{F}^{\top}\mathbf{F}+\lambda\mathbf{I}_{m}\Leftrightarrow\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}^{-1}=\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n},\quad\zeta\Leftrightarrow c_{1}^{2},\qquad\eta\Leftrightarrow c_{1}^{2}+c_{>1}^{2},

\displaystyle\tau_{1}=\frac{1}{m}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\mathbf{K}^{-1}\Leftrightarrow m_{1}=\frac{N}{n}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0},\quad\tau_{2}=\frac{1}{mn_{0}}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{K}^{-1}\Leftrightarrow m_{2}=\frac{N}{nd}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}.

N.6
Proof of Lemma
J.5

Define
\hat{\mathbf{X}}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}
, which implies
\hat{\mathbf{X}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}}
due to the Gaussianity of
\mathbf{X}
.
Based on the Gaussian equivalence from Appendix
I
,
we can replace

\mathbf{F}_{0}
with

c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
,
where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
is an independent random matrix with
{\sf N}(0,1)
entries, without changing the conclusion.
Hence, from now on, we write
\mathbf{F}_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
.
Further, we define

\displaystyle\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=c_{1}\hat{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}.

(89)

Thus,
by the definition of
\hat{\mathbf{X}}
,

\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=\mathbf{F}_{0}-c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})^{\top}
. As a consequence, we also have

\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}=\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top},

where
\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 2}
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\begin{bmatrix}0&c_{1}\\
c_{1}&c_{1}^{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}}\|_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix}.

Noting that
D
is invertible, and
using the Woodbury formula,
with
\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}
,
we find

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}.

(90)

Now, we can write

\displaystyle H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})=H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})-H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}}).

Next, we can analyze each term in the above sum separately.

The first term on the right hand side converges to zero by using Lemma
J.3
to prove the concentration of this term around its mean and noting that the mean is zero using the orthogonality property of Hermite polynomials (Lemma
C.1
).

To analyze the second term, we first study the matrix
\mathbf{K}=(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}
, writing

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}^{-1}=(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{u}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}}-\|\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}}\|_{2}^{2}&{\bm{u}}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}}-\frac{1}{c_{1}}\\
{\bm{u}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}}-\frac{1}{c_{1}}&{\bm{u}}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}}\end{bmatrix}.

It can readily verified that all elements in this matrix are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by checking the order of the operator and Euclidean norms.
Next, we analyze the terms in the expression

\displaystyle H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle=[\mathbf{K}]_{1,1}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+[\mathbf{K}]_{1,2}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+[\mathbf{K}]_{2,1}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+[\mathbf{K}]_{2,2}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}}).

Without loss of generality, we can assume that
p\neq 1
.

•

First Term.
Note that
H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}
and
H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})
are orthogonal in
L^{2}
by the properties of the Hermite polynomials,
and conditional on
{\bm{u}}
, they are independent of
\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
. Moreover,

\|\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/n)
.
Thus, by using Lemma
J.3
,
this term converges to zero.

•

Second Term.
Similar to the argument above, we can show that
(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})
converges to zero. Also, by analyzing the operator norms, we have
H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})=O(1)
. This implies that the second term converges to zero.

•

Third Term.
First, note that by a simple order-wise analysis,
H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Now, we have
H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})
is independent of
(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
and
\|(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{n})
. The term
(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})
converges to zero in probability by noting that
H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})
is mean zero for
p\neq 0
. For the
p=0
case, we can use an orthogonality invariance argument identical to the one used to analyze equation
33
.

•

Fourth Term.
This term also converges to zero because
(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}{\bm{u}})
converges to zero, as argued above.

Putting everything together, the proof is completed.

N.7
Proof of Lemma
J.6

We will prove part (a) first. To do this, we will first handle the cases where
p=0
and
p=1
.

For
p=0
, we have

\sqrt{N}H_{0}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=\sqrt{N}.
This is identical to the second term in equation
33
and it is shown to be
o_{{\mathbb{P}}}(1)

For
p=1
, we need to analyze

\sqrt{N}H_{1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=\sqrt{N}{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}.

Note that
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d})
is independent of
\sqrt{N}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}
and

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{N}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}\leq\sqrt{N}\|\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\|_{\rm op}\cdot\|\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\cdot\|\mathbf{F}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\cdot\|{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Thus, we can conclude that
\sqrt{N}H_{1}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\to 0
in probability.

To analyze the case where
p>1
, we first define
\hat{\mathbf{X}}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}
.
By construction, we have
\hat{\mathbf{X}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
J.5
,
Based on the Gaussian equivalence from Appendix
I
, we can replace
\mathbf{F}_{0}
with
c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
in our computations without changing the limiting result, where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
is an independent random matrix with
{\sf N}(0,1)
entries.
Thus, from now on, we denote
\mathbf{F}_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
.
We define
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}
as in equation
89
. Thus,
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=\mathbf{F}_{0}-c_{1}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}
. As a consequence, we can write
\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}=\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
,
where
\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}&\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 2}
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\begin{bmatrix}0&c_{1}\\
c_{1}&c_{1}^{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\|_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix}.

Using the Woodbury formula, we find
that equation
90
still holds.
Now, we can write

\displaystyle\sqrt{N}H_{p}

\displaystyle(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}

(91)

\displaystyle=\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}-\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}+c_{1}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}
