\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}+c_{1}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}){\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}.

Now, we can analyze each term in the above sum separately.

Term 1.

Note that by a simple orderwise analysis,

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{N}\,\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\|_{\rm op}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{N}\|\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\|\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\|_{\rm op}\|{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\|_{2}=O(1/\sqrt{N}).

We have
\|H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})
,
\mathbb{E}[H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})]=0
, and
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})
has independent entries. Also
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}
. Thus,

\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\to_{P}0
.

We now need to analyze
\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}
.
Note that
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
by a simple order analysis of the norms. We also have

\sqrt{N}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}\to_{P}0
,
because
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\sim{\sf N}(0,\frac{1}{d}\mathbf{I}_{d})
is independent of the norm bounded vector
\sqrt{N}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}
.

Term 2.

To analyze the second term, we first study the matrix
\mathbf{K}=(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}
:

\displaystyle\mathbf{K}^{-1}=(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})

\displaystyle=\begin{bmatrix}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\star}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\star}-\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}\|_{2}^{2}&{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\star}^{\top}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}-\frac{1}{c_{1}}\\
{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\star}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}-\frac{1}{c_{1}}&{\boldsymbol{\beta}}_{\star}^{\top}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}\end{bmatrix}.

By orderwise analysis, all elements in this matrix converge to deterministic
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
values in probability. We write the second term in equation
91
as follows:

\displaystyle\sqrt{N}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}

\displaystyle\mathbf{V}\mathbf{K}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}

\displaystyle=[\mathbf{K}]_{1,1}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})

\displaystyle+[\mathbf{K}]_{1,2}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})

\displaystyle+[\mathbf{K}]_{2,1}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2})

\displaystyle+[\mathbf{K}]_{2,2}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}(\sqrt{N}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}).

In the sum above, we will show that each term converges to zero.

•

First term
: By orderwise analysis, we have

\|\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})\|_{\rm op}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1/\sqrt{N})
.
Further,
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})
is independent of it (only considering the randomness in
\tilde{\mathbf{X}}
) with mean zero and
\|H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\|_{2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(\sqrt{N})
. This implies that

\displaystyle H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})\to_{P}0.

(92)

We can use a simple order argument to show that

\sqrt{N}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. Thus, the first term converges to zero.

•

Second term
: For this term, we use that
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})\to_{P}0
. We can also use an orderwise analysis to prove that

\sqrt{N}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

This proves that the second term also converges to zero.

•

Third term
: By a simple orderwise analysis, we have

\sqrt{N}(\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
To show that the third term converges to zero, it is enough to show that

H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\to_{P}0
,
which is true for
p\neq 1
by using Lemma
J.3
and the orthogonality property of Hermite polynomials (Lemma
C.1
).

•

Fourth term
: By a simple orderwise analysis, we have

\sqrt{N}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}{\bm{a}}^{\circ 2}=O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Again, to show that the fourth term converges to zero, it is enough to show that

H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\to_{P}0
,
which is true for
p\neq 1
as argued above.

Putting everything together,
part (a) follows.
The proof for part (b) is identical and omitted.

N.8
Proof of Lemma
J.7

We will study the cases where
s=1
and
s=2
separately. For
s=1
, we can use Lemma
J.1
to show that
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}=c_{\star,1}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Also, by Lemma
J.5
, we have
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})=o(1)
in probability if
p\neq 1
, which proves the lemma.

For the case
s=2
, we define
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}
and write

\displaystyle H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}})^{\circ 2}

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\;H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\circ 2}=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\;H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

Now, we define
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}-\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star},\,\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}-\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star},\,\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}}
, and set

\hat{\mathbf{X}}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\top}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}^{\top}
.
By construction, we have
\hat{\mathbf{X}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}
. Based on the Gaussian equivalence from Appendix
I
, we
can again replace
\mathbf{F}_{0}
with

\mathbf{F}_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
,
where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
is an independent random matrix with
{\sf N}(0,1)
entries.
Again, we define
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}
as in equation
89
.
Thus,
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=\mathbf{F}_{0}-c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}-c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp})^{\top}
. As a consequence, we also have

\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}=\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top},

where

\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 4}
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\begin{bmatrix}c_{1}^{2}\langle\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle&c_{1}^{2}\langle\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\rangle&c_{1}&0\vskip 5.69046pt\\
c_{1}^{2}\langle\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\rangle&c_{1}^{2}\langle\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp},\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\rangle&0&c_{1}\vskip 5.69046pt\\
c_{1}&0&0&0\vskip 5.69046pt\\
0&c_{1}&0&0\end{bmatrix}.

Using the Woodbury formula, we find that equation
90
still holds.
We can write

\displaystyle H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})=

\displaystyle H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

(93)

\displaystyle-H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}).

The first term converges to zero for any
p\neq 2
,
analogously to the argument in Section
J.2.1
for the term (1,2).

To prove that the second term will also converge to zero, we first observe that the elements of
\mathbf{K}=(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}
are all
O_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. The second term will involve quantities of the form

[\mathbf{K}]_{i,j}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{v}}_{i}{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
,
where
{\bm{v}}_{i}
, for
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, is the
i
-th column of the matrix
\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\end{bmatrix}
. We can argue that all these terms converge to zero, as follows:

•

The terms where
j=1
converge to zero because
(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

converges to zero
analogously to the argument in Section
J.2.1
for the term (1,2).
The same argument applies
to the terms where
j=2
, via the convergence of
(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
to zero.

•

For
j=3,4
,
since
H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
is independent of

\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}[\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\quad\,\,\,\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}]
,
and has zero-mean i.i.d. entries, it also follows
that these entries converge to zero in probability.

Finally we study
H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}
, by analyzing the terms in equation
93
for
p=2
.

For
H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
,
since

H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}),H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

are independent of
\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
,
it follows from Lemma
J.3
,
as in the analysis of term
(1,2)

in Section
J.2
,
that

H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\mathbb{E}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\cdot\mathbb{E}H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\to_{P}0
.
Now notice that
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}
is left-orthogonally invariant in distribution,
and thus

\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=_{d}\mathbf{O}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{O}^{\top}
,
where
\mathbf{O}

is uniformly distributed over the Haar measure of
n
-dimensional orthogonal matrices, independently of all other randomness.
Hence,

\mathbb{E}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{I}_{n}/n
.
Moreover, from the Woodbury formula in equation
24
,

\displaystyle|\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}|\leq|\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}|\leq|\operatorname{tr}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{V}|\cdot\|\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{op}}^{2}.

From our previous analysis and as the entries of
\mathbf{V}^{\top}\mathbf{V}
are
O_{\mathbb{P}}(n)
, it follows that the first term is
O_{\mathbb{P}}(n)
; whereas
\|\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\|_{\textnormal{op}}^{2}=O(1/n^{2})
. Hence,
|\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}|\to_{P}0
, and thus by the bounded convergence theorem
|\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}|\to_{P}0
.
Moreover, we have already argued
in the proof of Lemma
J.4

that
\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\to\psi m_{1}/\phi
.

Further, by Lemmas
C.1
and
J.1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=n\cdot\mathbb{E}H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{x}}_{1}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{x}}_{1}^{\top}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=2n\mathbb{E}(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{2}=2n\mathbb{E}\frac{(\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{2}}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|^{2}}=2n\frac{c_{\star,1}^{2}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

This shows that

H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\to_{P}2\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{2}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}.

Next,
we consider

H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}

with

\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\end{bmatrix}
.
For the first two entries of the vector

H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}
,
an analysis very similar to the one above
for

H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

shows that they converge to zero in probability.
For the last two entries,
since
H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})
is independent of

\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}[\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\quad\,\,\,\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}]
,
and has zero-mean i.i.d. entries, it also follows
that these entries converge to zero in probability.
Moreover,
the limiting entries of

(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}

have been shown to be bounded in our above analysis.
Hence, the second term
converges to zero in probability.

Now, note that
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}
. From Lemma
J.1
,

\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|^{2}\to_{P}\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}
.
Hence,

H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{2}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})=2\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi}\frac{c_{\star,1}^{2}\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}}{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)\to_{P}\frac{2c_{\star,1}^{2}\psi m_{1}}{\phi},

which concludes the proof.

N.9
Proof of Lemma
J.8

As in the proof of Lemma
J.6
,
we define

\hat{\mathbf{X}}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\top}
.
By construction, we have
\hat{\mathbf{X}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
J.5
,
based on the Gaussian equivalence from Appendix
I
, we can replace
\mathbf{F}_{0}
with
c_{1}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
in our computations without changing the limiting result, where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
is an independent random matrix with
{\sf N}(0,1)
entries.
Thus, from now on, we denote
\mathbf{F}_{0}=c_{1}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
.
We define
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}
as in equation
89
; thus,
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=\mathbf{F}_{0}-c_{1}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\top}
. As a consequence, we can write
\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}=\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
,
where
\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}&\tilde{\bm{\theta}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 2}
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\begin{bmatrix}0&c_{1}\\
c_{1}&c_{1}^{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix}.

Using the Woodbury formula, we find
that equation
90
still holds.
Now, we can write

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}

\displaystyle=\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}-\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}.

(94)

We can analyze each term in the above sum separately.

By Lemma
J.3
,
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}-\mathbb{E}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\to_{P}0
.
Further,
conditional on
\boldsymbol{\beta}
,

\mathbb{E}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}=3\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{4}\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
;
and as in the proof of Lemma
J.7
,

\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\to 0
.
Moreover, we have already argued
in the proof of Lemma
J.4

that
\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\to\psi m_{1}/\phi
.
In addition, from Lemma
J.1
,

\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|^{2}\to_{P}\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}
.
Hence,

\displaystyle\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2}\to_{P}3\psi m_{1}[\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}]^{2}/\phi.

To analyze the second term in equation
94
, we first study

\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}
.
By an argument similar to the ones above, we can show that it concentrates around

\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}=\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}
.
Since
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}
is left-orthogonally invariant,

\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}=_{d}\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}
, where
\mathbf{O}

is uniformly distributed over the Haar measure of
n
-dimensional orthogonal matrices, independently of all other randomness.
Then, it follows
as in the analysis of term (1,2) from Section
J.2
that
\mathbf{1}_{n}^{\top}\mathbf{O}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}0
;
and hence
\tilde{\bm{\theta}}^{\circ 2\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}\to_{P}0
.

Moreover,
the limiting entries of

(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}

can be shown to be bounded by a simple orderwise analysis. Hence, the second term in equation
94
is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.

N.10
Proof of Lemma
K.2

Denoting
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}=\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}
, we have

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ i\top}

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ j}=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{i+j}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\circ i\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\circ j}=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{i+j}\sum_{k_{1}=0}^{i}\sum_{k_{2}=0}^{j}\xi_{i,k_{1}}\xi_{j,k_{2}}H_{k_{1}}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{k_{2}}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{i+j}\sum_{k=0}^{\min(i,j)}\xi_{j,k}\xi_{i,k}H_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)

\displaystyle=\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{i+j}\left[\xi_{i,1}\xi_{j,1}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})+\sum_{k=0,\;k\neq 1}^{\min(i,j)}\xi_{i,k}\xi_{j,k}H_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{k}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\right]+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1).

The third line follows from Lemma
J.5
. Now, we claim that for any
p\in\{0,2,3,\dots\}
, we have
H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2})\to_{P}p!\;\psi m_{1}/\phi
.
Using this claim, the facts that
\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}^{2}\to_{P}c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})
, and
\operatorname{tr}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}^{\top}(\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}+\lambda n\mathbf{I}_{n})^{-1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}})/d\to_{P}\psi m_{2}/\phi
, we can conclude

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ i\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})^{\circ j}\to_{P}\left(c_{\star,1}^{2}+\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})\right)^{(i+j)/2}\left[\xi_{i,1}\xi_{j,1}\frac{\psi m_{2}}{\phi}+\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi}\sum_{k=0,\;k\neq 1}^{\min(i,j)}k!\;\xi_{i,k}\xi_{j,k}\right].

Now, it remains to prove the claim that for any
p\in\{0,2,3,\dots\}
, we have

\displaystyle H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}/\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2})\to_{P}p!\;\psi m_{1}/\phi.

As in the proof of Lemma
J.8
,
we define

\hat{\mathbf{X}}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\top}
.
By construction, we have
\hat{\mathbf{X}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
.
As in the proof of Lemma
J.5
,
based on the Gaussian equivalence from Appendix
I
, we can replace
\mathbf{F}_{0}
with
c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
in our computations without changing the limiting result, where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
is an independent random matrix with
{\sf N}(0,1)
entries.
Thus, from now on, we denote
\mathbf{F}_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
.
We define
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}
as in equation
89
; thus,
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=\mathbf{F}_{0}-c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}
. As a consequence, we can write
\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}=\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top}
,
where
\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 2}
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\begin{bmatrix}0&c_{1}\\
c_{1}&c_{1}^{2}\|\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}^{2}\end{bmatrix}.

Using the Woodbury formula, we find
that equation
90
still holds.
Now, we can write

\displaystyle H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}

\displaystyle)^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})=H_{p}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-H_{p}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}.

(95)

We can analyze each term in the above sum separately.

By Lemma
J.3
,
H_{p}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\mathbb{E}H_{p}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\to_{P}0
.
Further,
conditional on
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
, and using
C.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}H_{p}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\left[\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})H_{p}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}^{\top}\right]=p!\;\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\left[\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\right],

and as in the proof of Lemma
J.7
,

\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\to 0
.
Moreover, we have already argued
in the proof of Lemma
J.4

that
\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\to\psi m_{1}/\phi
. Hence,

H_{p}{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\to_{P}p!\;\psi m_{1}/\phi.

To analyze the second term in equation
N.10
, we first study

H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}
.
Conditional on
\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
,

H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
is a vector
with independent mean-zero, bounded variance entries,
independent of the vector
\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}
that has norm
O(1/\sqrt{n})
.
Hence, we conclude that this term goes to zero.
Next, note that
H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\to_{P}0
using Lemma
J.3
and Lemma
C.1
. Moreover,
the limiting entries of

(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}

can be shown to be bounded by a simple orderwise analysis. Hence, the second term in equation
N.10
is
o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
. This concludes the proof.

N.11
Proof of Lemma
K.3

We define
\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}-\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star},\,\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star}-\langle\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star},\,\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\|_{2}}
, and set

\hat{\mathbf{X}}=\tilde{\mathbf{X}}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}^{\top}-\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}^{\top}
.
By construction, we have
\hat{\mathbf{X}}\mathchoice{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\displaystyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\displaystyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\textstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\textstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptstyle\perp}}}{\mathrel{\hbox to0.0pt{$\scriptscriptstyle\perp$\hss}\mkern 2.0mu{\scriptscriptstyle\perp}}}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}
. Based on the Gaussian equivalence from Appendix
I
, we
can again replace
\mathbf{F}_{0}
with

\mathbf{F}_{0}=c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\mathbf{W}_{0}^{\top}+c_{>1}\mathbf{Z}
,
where
\mathbf{Z}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times d}
is an independent random matrix with
{\sf N}(0,1)
entries.
Again, we define
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}
as in equation
89
.
Thus,
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}=\mathbf{F}_{0}-c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}-c_{1}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}(\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp})^{\top}
. As a consequence, we also have

\mathbf{F}_{0}\mathbf{F}_{0}^{\top}=\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}^{\top}+\mathbf{V}\mathbf{D}\mathbf{V}^{\top},

where

\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\end{bmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times 4}
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}=\begin{bmatrix}c_{1}^{2}\langle\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\rangle&c_{1}^{2}\langle\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\rangle&c_{1}&0\vskip 5.69046pt\\
c_{1}^{2}\langle\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}},\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\rangle&c_{1}^{2}\langle\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp},\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\rangle&0&c_{1}\vskip 5.69046pt\\
c_{1}&0&0&0\vskip 5.69046pt\\
0&c_{1}&0&0\end{bmatrix}.

Using the Woodbury formula, we find that equation
90
still holds.
We can write

\displaystyle H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}

\displaystyle H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}).

(96)

p\neq q
case:

The first term converges to zero for any
p\neq q
,
analogously to the argument in Section
J.2.1
for the terms (1,2) and (2,4).
In particular, for
p=0
, we can use orthogonal invariance as in the analysis of the term (2,4). To prove that the second term will also converge to zero when
p\neq q
, we first observe that the elements of
\mathbf{K}=(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}
are all
O(1)
. The second term will involve quantities of the form

[\mathbf{K}]_{i,j}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}{\bm{v}}_{i}{\bm{v}}_{j}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
,
where
{\bm{v}}_{i}
, for
i\in\{1,2,3,4\}
, is the
i
-th column of the matrix
\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\end{bmatrix}
. We can argue that all these terms converge to zero, as follows. Because
p\neq q
, without loss of generality, assume that
q\neq 1
.

•

The terms where
j=1
converge to zero because
(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

converges to zero using the concentration argument from Lemma
J.3
and the orthogonality of Hermite polynomials from Lemma
C.1
.
The same argument applies
to the terms where
j=2
, via the convergence of
(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
to zero.

•

For
j=3,4
,
and for
q>0
,
since
H_{q}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
is independent of

\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}[\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\quad\,\,\,\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}]
,
and has zero-mean i.i.d. entries, it also follows
that these entries converge to zero in probability.
For
q=0
, we can again use orthogonal invariance as in the analysis of the term (2,4).

The case when
p=q\neq 1
:

Finally we study
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
, by analyzing the terms in equation
93
.

For
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})
,
since

H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star}),H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

are independent of
\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}
,
it follows from Lemma
J.3
,
as in the analysis of term
(1,2)

in the Section
J.2
,
that

H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})-\mathbb{E}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\cdot\mathbb{E}H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\to_{P}0.

Now notice that
\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}
is left-orthogonally invariant in distribution,
and thus

\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=_{d}\mathbf{O}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{O}^{\top}
,
where
\mathbf{O}

is uniformly distributed over the Haar measure of
n
-dimensional orthogonal matrices, independently of all other randomness.
Hence,

\mathbb{E}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}=\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{I}_{n}/n
.
Also, similar to the proof of Lemma
J.7
, we have
|\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}-\operatorname{tr}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}|=o_{\mathbb{P}}(1)
.
Moreover, we have already argued
in the proof of Lemma
J.4

that
\mathbb{E}\operatorname{tr}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\to\psi m_{1}/\phi
. Further, by Lemmas
C.1
and
J.1
,

H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})\to_{P}p!\;\frac{\psi m_{1}}{\phi}\left(\frac{c_{\star,1}}{\sqrt{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}}\right)^{p}.

Next,
we consider

H_{2}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}

with

\mathbf{V}=\begin{bmatrix}\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}&\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}\end{bmatrix}
.
For the first two entries of the vector

H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V}
,
an analysis very similar to the one above
for

H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}H_{p}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

shows that they converge to zero in probability.
For the last two entries,
since
H_{p}(\tilde{\bm{\theta}}_{\star})
is independent of

\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}[\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}}\quad\,\,\,\hat{\mathbf{F}}_{0}\mathbf{W}_{0}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\perp}]
,
and has zero-mean i.i.d. entries, it also follows
that these entries converge to zero in probability.
Moreover,
the limiting entries of

(\mathbf{D}^{-1}+\mathbf{V}^{\top}\hat{\mathbf{R}}_{0}\mathbf{V})^{-1}

have been shown to be bounded in our above analysis.
Hence, the second term
converges to zero in probability.

The case when
p=q=1
:

In this case, we have

\displaystyle(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\tilde{\boldsymbol{\beta}})

\displaystyle=\frac{(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta}_{\star})^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{R}}_{0}(\tilde{\mathbf{X}}\boldsymbol{\beta})}{\|\boldsymbol{\beta}\|_{2}}=\frac{c_{\star,1}\frac{\psi m_{2}}{\phi}}{\sqrt{\phi(c_{\star}^{2}+\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+c_{\star,1}^{2}}}+o_{\mathbb{P}}(1),

using Lemma
J.1
and by arguments similar to the ones in the proof of Lemma
J.4
.

Putting everything together concludes the proof.
